## 2026-10-04T07:08:44Z · p02-tenancy · round 2 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `183fd57b6f775ea4b23de5faed9515c0984d54ea`
- Critic: fresh critic, p02-tenancy round 2
- Biggest gap: **Company access is a grant that nothing guards: a user with only 'change company access' locks the Administrator out of a company, and no gate looks** — PUT /api/tenancy/access/{userId} never checks the target against the caller. It ignores what the target holds, and it ignores the caller's own branch limits. A clerk whose role holds only tenancy.access.read and tenancy.access.update removed the tenant Administrator from ALN-DXB. The Administrator cannot undo it, because callers may not change their own access. A manager limited to one branch can also give someone every branch of the company. For a UAE group running several legal entities (mainland LLC, free-zone FZE), this means a junior can take an owner or accountant out of an entity's books. Odoo restricts company-access edits to Settings/Administration rights. The gates miss this whole class: G2's takeover check covers only /api/identity/users/{..}, and GrantEscalation knows only roleIds and permissions. The company attack also never completes a write: its bodies fail e-mail validation, so planted cross-company write C2 passes. With no reviewed permission map for /api/tenancy, planted P1 (logo replacement under tenancy.workplace.switch) passes too. The fix: treat company and branch access as a grant (a caller may only give or take access they hold, to and from users who hold no more than they do), add /api/tenancy to the takeover, grant-escalation and reviewed-map gates, and make the company attack send valid bodies.
- Gap evidence: attack.log, section '== Junior holding only tenancy.access.update (+read) strips the administrator ==': 'junior removes the administrator from ALN-DXB 200', then 'administrator now sees companies ALN-AUH,ALN-FZE,ALN-SHJ', then 'administrator tries to restore own access 400'. db-inspection.txt shows the delete row with actor 01a10598-6e18-... (the junior). The branch-manager case: 'branch manager gives the junior ALL branches of ALN-DXB 200'. To reproduce on ./erp up: as admin@alnoor.example, POST /api/identity/roles {permissions:['tenancy.access.read','tenancy.access.update']}, create a user with that role, PUT /api/tenancy/access/{user} with ALN-DXB, sign in as that user, then PUT /api/tenancy/access/{administrator id} {companies:[]}. Gate misses: plants/C2.diff with C2-gates.log and C2-probe.log; plants/P1.diff with P1-g2.log.
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation: suite and hand attacks: **PASS** — G1 HTTP, database, process-state, source, client and non-interference tests all pass in ./erp verify (verify.log). Hand attacks (attack.log): signed in as alnoor's administrator against gulfsteel, I tried GET/PUT of B's company, logo and branch, POST of a branch into B's company, a workplace switch to B, a grant of B's company, a filter by B's companyId and a read of B's user's access. Every answer was 404, 400 or an empty list. erp_app has neither superuser nor BYPASSRLS. Every tenancy table has tenant_isolation plus a RESTRICTIVE company_scope policy (db-inspection.txt). No real cross-tenant leak was found.
  - G1 tenant isolation: planted tenant leak in this piece (T1): **PASS** — Plant T1 (plants/apply.py T1, all-plants.diff) adds a static logo cache in CompanyEndpoints.GetLogo. Two gates caught it: G1ProcessStateTests ('static ...CompanyEndpoints.LogoCache ... mutable ConcurrentDictionary') and G1HttpIsolationTests ('18 leaks: GET /api/tenancy/companies/{B}/logo -> 200 contains tenant B marker') (plants/gates-all-plants.log).
  - G1 company layer (company-scoping attacks, this piece's own gate): **FAIL** — Plant C2 (plants/C2.diff): POST /api/tenancy/branches calls ErpDbSession.IncludeNewCompanyAsync(request.CompanyId) before writing. This is a real leak. On the planted host, x.admin (Administrator role, ALN-DXB only) gets 404 for ALN-SHJ but POST /branches {companyId: ALN-SHJ} answers 201 with companyCode 'ALN-SHJ'. The unplanted demo answers 400 unknownIds (plants/C2-probe.log). Yet G1CompanyIsolationTests, G1CompanyScopeTests, G1TenantSourceTests and G1ReviewedLookupTests all PASS with it in place (plants/C2-gates.log). The cause: the attack's generated body puts 'company-attack-N' in email, which the validator refuses (400 'Enter a valid e-mail address.', reproduced in C2-probe.log), so the attack never completes a create or update on branches. (Plant C1, a stale workplace kept in scope, turned out inert: user_workplaces cascades from user_company_access. It is not counted.)
  - G2 permissions: planted action without its permission (P1): **FAIL** — Plant P1 (plants/P1.diff): PUT /api/tenancy/companies/{id}/logo is guarded by tenancy.workplace.switch instead of tenancy.companies.update, so anyone who may switch company can replace company logos. Every G2 test passes with it in place (23/23, plants/P1-g2.log), and so do the tenancy module tests (21/21, P1-tenancy.log). tests/Gates/endpoint-permissions/ has a reviewed map for identity only. Nothing reviews the permission each /api/tenancy endpoint should declare.
  - G2 permissions: in-handler check removed (P2) and access takeover: **FAIL** — Plant P2 (callers may change their own company access) passes every gate. Only the module test AccessTests.Administrators_give_only_their_own_companies_and_never_change_their_own_access catches it (plants/tenancy-tests.log). There is also a real, unplanted problem: G2AccountTakeoverTests covers only /api/identity/users/{..}, and GrantEscalation knows only the roleIds and permissions fields. So PUT /api/tenancy/access/{userId} is never checked against what the target holds. A user whose role holds only tenancy.access.read and tenancy.access.update removed the Administrator from ALN-DXB (200), and the Administrator could not restore it (400). It is audited as a delete by that user (attack.log, db-inspection.txt).

Ports and the compose project are overridable from the environment as documented. Testcontainers ports are still random, as earlier critics noted.
  - Rule gates: licences, decimal money, English/Arabic strings, audit: **PASS** — The string check reports 497 keys in English and Arabic. The licence, money and audit rule gates pass in the suite. Every user write to a tenancy table leaves an audit row with its actor (companies, branches, user_company_access, user_branch_access, user_workplaces; db-inspection.txt). The focus does not move to the branch line after saving, which costs a click. The shipped ours driver was a stub, so I wrote it (drivers/create-company-branch.mjs). - Other findings:
  - Within one tenant, company codes leak across companies through writes. An administrator of ALN-DXB only gets 409 'duplicate' when creating a company coded ALN-SHJ, which they cannot see, and 201 for a code that exists nowhere (attack.log). The company attack has no write-oracle check.

  - [route: p01-odoo-rig] Under the strict per-metric rule a keystroke floor tie cannot be won: 0=0 for a one-click switch, and 72=72 when both products type only the required names. Both p02 tasks therefore lose on keys while winning steps and time by 40 to 50 percent. Blindness is also incomplete: the company list in the blind shots shows 'Al Noor ...' company names, and only the workspace name is masked (shot 07).
  - Arabic: the company form header shows the English legal name (code · legalNameEn) on Arabic screens (shot 05). A company or branch can be saved with no Arabic name, so the 'Falcon Logistics LLC' company created in the comparison has none.

## 2026-10-04T14:38:11Z · p02-tenancy · round 3 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `d43be04bb9b681f33901e479135584a6fb3e3b43`
- Critic: fresh critic, p02-tenancy round 3
- Biggest gap: **The new company and branch grant rules are guarded only by hand-written module tests: the gates miss three of the planted rule faults, including round 2's takeover** — The product now holds every rule I attacked by hand. A clerk cannot strip the Administrator, a one-company administrator gets the same 403 for a hidden code and an unused code, and a one-branch administrator sees and changes only their branch. The gates that are supposed to cover new endpoints automatically do not check these rules independently. (1) G2CompanyAccessGrantTests passes with the 'target holds permissions the caller lacks' check removed (plant P3). Its only junior works in one company, so the 'target works in companies you do not' rule refuses first. On the planted host, a clerk who works in every company removed the tenant Administrator from ALN-DXB, which is round 2's biggest gap back with every gate green. (2) G1CompanyIsolationTests passes with the 'needs every company' check removed from company create (plant C4). Its write oracle sends only the first three 'code' values of company Y, and those are branch codes, so it never sends Y's company code to POST /companies. The planted host answers 409 for a hidden company's code and 201 for an unused one. (3) No gate attacks branch scope: with the branch filter switched off (plant C3), a one-branch administrator lists and renames the company's other branches. Each fault is caught only by a hand-written test in Erp.Modules.Tenancy.Tests, not by a G1 or G2 gate that would also cover the next module's company- and branch-owned tables. Tenancy screens also have no screen permission gate (plants U1 and U2 pass). For a UAE group running a mainland LLC and a free-zone FZE, these rules are what keep a junior from removing an owner's access to an entity's books. The fix: in the grant gate, test each refusal with an attacker for whom only that rule can apply (for example, a clerk in every company); make the company write oracle send company Y's value for each identifying column of every table, not the first three values found; add a branch-limited attacker to the company attack; and give the tenancy screens the same plant-tested permission gate the identity screens have.
- Gap evidence: plants/P3.diff + plants/P3-gates.log (G2CompanyAccessGrantTests Passed) + plants/P3-tenancy-module.log (only GrantRulesTests fails). plants/batchB.diff (T2, C4, C3) + plants/batchB-gates.log (G1CompanyIsolationTests Passed, only process state failed for T2) + plants/batchB-tenancy-module.log. Real-fault proof on a host built with plants/host-P3-C4-C3.diff over the demo database (critic-scripts/probe-planted.mjs): plants/planted-probe-2.log shows 'clerk removes the tenant Administrator from ALN-DXB 200' and 'administrator companies before 5 after ALN-AUH,ALN-FZE,ALN-SHJ'. plants/planted-probe.log shows 'x creates code ALN-SHJ 409' vs 'Z221656 201', and the one-branch administrator listing 'AQZ-WH,DEIRA-HQ,DIP-SR'. The unplanted control answers 403 userBeyondOwn, 403/403 and 404 in the same files. To reproduce: python3 plants/apply.py <clone> P3 && dotnet build && dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests --filter FullyQualifiedName~G2CompanyAccessGrantTests passes.
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation: suite and hand attacks: **PASS** — All G1 tests pass in ./erp verify (verify.log): HTTP isolation (1 h 3 m under load), database, process state, source, non-interference and company isolation. I ran hand attacks as alnoor's administrator against gulfsteel (attack.log section 1). GET, PUT and DELETE on B's company, logo and branch, POST of a branch into B, a workplace switch to B's company or branch, a grant of B's company or branch, GET and PUT on B's administrator's access, a branch filter by B's companyId, a search by B's code and an X-Tenant header all answered 404, 400 or an empty list. B's company version was 773 before and after. db-inspection.txt: erp_app, erp_owner and erp_auth have no SUPERUSER and no BYPASSRLS. Every tenancy table has RLS enabled and forced, with tenant_isolation, plus a RESTRICTIVE company_scope on the company tables. erp_app with no tenant set sees 0 rows everywhere. ./erp tenant create, list and suspend worked (tenant-cli.log). I found no real cross-tenant leak.
  - G1 tenant isolation: planted tenant leaks in this piece (T1 database policy, T2 logo cache): **PASS** — T1 (plants/batchA.diff) adds a permissive SELECT policy on tenancy.user_company_totals. G1DatabaseIsolationTests caught it: 'tenancy.user_company_totals: tenant A sees 3 rows of other tenants', and the standard-policy test failed too (plants/batchA-gates.log). T2 (plants/batchB.diff) adds a static logo cache. G1ProcessStateTests caught it (plants/batchB-gates.log).
  - G1 company layer: planted cross-company write (C2) and in-tenant company-code oracle (C4): **FAIL** — C2 (IncludeNewCompanyAsync in branch create) is caught now. G1CompanyIsolationTests reports '113 leaks of company Y: company X administrator -> POST /api/tenancy/branches -> 201' (batchA-gates.log), so the round-2 gap stays fixed. C4 is not caught. It removes the 'needs every company' check from POST /api/tenancy/companies (plants/batchB.diff), and G1CompanyIsolationTests still PASSES with it in place (batchB-gates.log, 15 m 25 s; it ran only 5 write-oracle checks). It is a real oracle. On a host built with the plant over the demo database, an administrator of ALN-DXB alone gets 409 'duplicate' for code ALN-SHJ, which they cannot see, and 201 for a code that exists nowhere. The unplanted demo answers 403 to both (plants/planted-probe.log). ## 2026-10-05T01:21:52Z · p00-foundation · round 5 · LOSS

- Commit judged: `eab97e55efe6d1d46ed4a6e97dffddc3cd339a2a`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 5
- Biggest gap: **Sign-in to an empty workspace ties Odoo (steps, keystrokes, modelled human time); a tie is a loss** — The only task p00 is compared on is 'sign in to an empty workspace'. Our sign-in screen only beats Odoo when it remembers the e-mail and focuses the password field (the returning-user path). An empty/first-time workspace has no remembered e-mail, so both products need the identical shortest expert path: type e-mail, Tab, type password, Enter. That is 4 steps, 57 keystrokes and 19.06 modelled human seconds in each product, so the task ties on every human-facing metric and ours wins only machine seconds, which the owner's bar does not count as a win ('a tie is a loss'). To actually beat Odoo on the empty-workspace sign-in, reduce the human path itself for a first visit (for example: a single combined credential submit, passkey/WebAuthn as the default, a magic-link or one-tap that removes the password field, or autofocus plus Enter-to-submit from the e-mail field that skips the Tab) rather than relying on a remembered e-mail that does not exist on an empty workspace.
- Gap evidence: Committed Odoo baseline: gauntlet/reference/odoo/tasks/sign-in.json -> counts.steps=4, keystrokes=57, human_seconds=19.06. Our fresh-device path is documented at 4/57/19.06 by the r3 and r4 verdicts (gauntlet/verdicts/p00-foundation--r003/r004). Reproduce: node gauntlet/compare/run.mjs --task sign-in --product both --out <out> against a freshly seeded ./erp up (never previously signed in on that browser profile) and the odoo-reference rig; compare steps/keystrokes/klm human seconds.

## 2026-10-05T19:06:27Z · p06-form-report · round 1 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `8bf468ff33ebdd59a9df1be8128402d2b48d3fca`
- Critic: fresh critic, p06-form-report round 1
- Biggest gap: **The isolation gate is blind to the print/export surface: a print store shared across tenants leaks and every gate passes** — Printing and export are where a UAE trading company's data leaves the system (PDFs to vendors, Excel to accountants), and this piece owns that surface. A print store that ignores the tenant (here: files kept on disk by download name, the same 'Roles 2026-10-05.pdf' for every tenant) hands tenant B's users list and roles PDF to tenant A. Yet all 110 gate tests pass, G1HttpIsolation, non-interference and the reports isolation probe included. The probe attacks only as tenant A, and the victim's background activity never prints or exports. So nothing tenant B prints is ever there for A to receive. The gate needs tenant B to print and export every report and list (each format, both languages, same and different queries) before and during A's attack, and must judge A's PDFs, CSVs and workbooks for B's markers. Related: G2 accepts any permission a report definition names, so a report that prints companies under tenancy.workplace.read passes (plant P1).
- Gap evidence: plants/plant-L2-printed-files-on-disk.patch applied to 8bf468f → tests/Erp.Gates.Tests passes 110/110 (plants/run-L2.log, toolbox container, filter Load!=Timing&Process!=self-*). Live on the planted stack: GET /api/reports/lists/identity.users?format=csv as admin@alnoor, then the same as admin@gulfsteel → gulfsteel receives 304 lines of @alnoor.example users. /api/reports/lists/identity.roles?format=pdf&language=ar comes back byte-identical (plants/plant-L2-live-demo.txt). The harness is in scripts/run-gates.sh.
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation (product as built): **PASS** — The gate suite passed inside verify (G1HttpIsolation 9 min, company isolation, non-interference, process state). I attacked as admin@gulfsteel against alnoor on the live demo: companyProfile with each of alnoor's 4 company ids in json/pdf/csv/xlsx × en/ar with X-Tenant-Id gave 404 every time. branchDirectory and usersByRole with alnoor ids gave 0 rows and 'Not found'. All 5 printable lists, searched for alnoor names, codes and Arabic text in csv and json with X-Tenant-Id/X-Company-Id headers, gave 0 alnoor values (the only hits were the attacker's own name). Company logo paths gave 404. Filters on alnoor company ids were refused (400). Database: erp_app has no BYPASSRLS and no superuser, and sees 0 company rows unbound and none of alnoor's when bound to gulfsteel. Details: attack-api.txt.
  - G1 catches faults planted in p06's surface: **FAIL** — Plant L2 (plants/plant-L2-printed-files-on-disk.patch) keeps printed PDF/CSV/XLSX files under /tmp/erp-printed, named by the download name (title + date + language + format), and serves them again. The whole gate project still PASSED: 110/110 (plants/run-L2.log). On a live stack the leak is real (plants/plant-L2-live-demo.txt): alnoor's admin prints the users CSV, then gulfsteel's admin prints its own and gets alnoor's file, 304 alnoor lines. The roles Arabic PDF comes back byte-identical. Likely cause: the victim tenant never prints or exports (format=pdf/csv/xlsx) before the attacker, so a print-side store fed by tenant B is never exercised. Plant L1 (an in-memory static cache of printed list documents) was caught three ways: process state, the HTTP attack (4,500 leaks) and the company oracle (plants/run-L1-P1-P2.log).
  - G2 permissions (product as built): **PASS** — The suite passes. viewer sees the company form read-only: disabled fields, no Save, the reason shown. The catalogue lists only the reports the caller may run.
  - G2 catches faults planted in p06's surface: **FAIL** — Plant P1 (plants/plant-P1-report-wrong-permission.patch) puts the company profile report, with licence, TRN, address and branches, under tenancy.workplace.read instead of tenancy.companies.read. Plant P2 (plants/plant-P2-catalog-unfiltered.patch) removes the permission filter from /api/reports/catalog. With both applied, all 24 G2 tests PASSED (plants/run-L1-P1-P2.log; only the 3 G1 tests failed, for L1). G2 only checks that a report route declares the permission its own definition names; nothing ties a report's permission to the data its source reads. Web side: plant P3a (the kernel form ignores canEdit) was caught by 8 unit tests. P3b (the company form always editable) passed all 175 unit tests (plants/plant-P3-web.log); tenancy.spec.ts:94-95 looks as if it would catch it in e2e, but I did not run that.
  - G3 clean clone: **PASS** — Fresh clone at 8bf468f. `ERP_PROJECT=c-p06-form-report-r1 ERP_HTTP_PORT=20650 ERP_DB_PORT=20651 ERP_VERIFY_HTTP_PORT=20660 ERP_VERIFY_DB_PORT=20661 ./erp verify` passed in 966 s, ratchet passed (verify.log). `./erp up` gave the seeded demo (100,000 users) in 36 s (up.log). Port and project overrides worked.
  - Rule gates (licence, money, strings, audit, reports): **PASS** — These pass inside verify. The fonts are OFL-1.1, which the owner approved on 2026-10-03 (needs-human #5), each listed with its sha256. A company save through the form writes audit.entries with the actor and old/new phone (checked in the database).
- Tests: 750 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped of 750 (`ERP_PROJECT=c-p06-form-report-r1 ERP_HTTP_PORT=20650 ERP_DB_PORT=20651 ERP_VERIFY_HTTP_PORT=20660 ERP_VERIFY_DB_PORT=20661 ./erp verify`, 966s)
- Clean clone to running demo: PASS in 966s — One command from a fresh clone: 370 .NET + 175 web unit + 59 e2e + 146 compare tests, all passed. Each count equals its ratchet minimum, none fell. `./erp up` gave a running seeded demo in 36 s.
- Odoo side by side:

  | Task | Ours steps / keys / s / human s | Odoo steps / keys / s / human s | Winner | Notes |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | edit-and-save | 3 / 17 / 0.126 / 9.72 | 4 / 19 / 0.18 / 10.28 | ours | Harness verdict: win, median of 3 verified runs per product. Ours: click the phone field (the click selects the whole number) > type > Ctrl+S. Odoo: click > Ctrl+A > type > Alt+S. The records differ: ours edits a company (ALN-DXB) because contacts (p16) are not built; Odoo edits a contact. |
  | arabic-report | 2 / 0 / 0.083 / 5.3 | 6 / 0 / 3.448 / 15.9 | odoo | Harness verdict: loss. Keystrokes tie at 0 and 0, and a tie is a loss. Ours wins steps (2 vs 6), machine seconds (0.08 vs 3.4) and modelled human seconds (5.3 vs 15.9). Ours prints the company profile through Print > PDF in Arabic without switching the interface language; Odoo has to switch the user to Arabic before its purchase order prints right to left. No path can go below 0 keystrokes, so this task cannot be won while Odoo's path also has none. The piece spec names 'print a list report in Arabic', but no harness task covers list printing: this one prints a single record's document. |

- Other findings:
  - Arabic PDFs print phone numbers backwards: '+971 2 555 7810' becomes '7810 555 2 971+' in the company profile (08-pdf-company-profile-ar.jpg, Phone row and branch rows). The same document on screen shows them in the right order (07). Numbers made of runs separated by spaces need an LTR isolate in the PDF bidi pass (Pdf/Bidi.cs).
  - The Arabic PDF's text layer maps glyphs back to the wrong letters, so copy, search and screen readers get wrong words: الاسم → 'الإشم', الرمز → 'الرمر', العربية → 'العرنية' (company-profile-ar-extracted-text.txt, extracted with pdf.js from company-profile-ar.pdf). Most likely the ToUnicode map gives one character to glyphs shared by letters such as س/ش and ر/ز.
  - Malformed PDF header: every PDF starts '%PDF-1.7m' (src/Modules/Reports/Erp.Modules.Reports/Pdf/PdfWriter.cs:90). pdf.js warns 'Invalid PDF header version: 1.7m'; strict validators and archivers may refuse the file.
  - Totals are not visible anywhere: no registered report or printable list has a totalled column (catalogue: total=false on every column of all 3 reports and 5 lists), so a user sees only group row counts. The money and decimal form fields are used by no screen, and date and lookup appear only as report parameters. Scope that exists only in components and unit tests.
  - The list Print or export menu offers CSV and Excel in English only, even to an Arabic user (hrefs carry language=en). The API accepts language=ar.
  - The list print button has no keyboard shortcut (no aria-keyshortcuts); the record form has Alt+R. Keyboard-first users must tab to it.
  - The 'print a list report in Arabic' comparison named in the piece spec has no harness task; arabic-report prints a single record's document. A list-printing task should be added. [route: p01-odoo-rig]
  - Harness: a metric where both products score 0 (keystrokes in arabic-report) is a tie and therefore a loss, so the task cannot be won whatever ours does. That is the owner's rule, but worth surfacing to the owner. [route: p01-odoo-rig]
  - G2 on screen covers identity screens only (identity-plant-self-test.mjs). Plant P3b (company form editable for a read-only user) passes all 175 web unit tests and is left to a single e2e assertion. [route: p02-tenancy]

## 2026-10-07T00:00:32Z · p04-shell · round 4 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `3b3b0b6a533db433634afb9599ecbe292aa35a93`
- Critic: fresh critic, p04-shell round 4
- Biggest gap: **The isolation gate never exercises the Arabic side of the shell: a cross-tenant leak behind Arabic-Indic digits (or language=ar) on the shell's own endpoint passes every gate** — G1's write-after-write and non-interference phases build every write body from the first documented enum value. TenantActivity.OwnLeaf hard-codes language 'en' and numerals 'latn', and OpenApiDocument.BuildBody(useDocumentedValues) returns enum[0]. So no tenant pair ever sends language 'ar' or numerals 'arab', and the code paths behind them are never attacked. Those are this piece's own features and half of the UAE market's users. Any state that leaks between tenants only on the Arabic path (a cache keyed by language, a per-locale formatter memo, a shared file or a header set only for Arabic) ships unseen. Fix (gate machinery in TenantActivity/OpenApiDocument, owned with p00): for every write endpoint, run the tenant-B-then-tenant-A write pairs and the non-interference comparison once per allowed value of every enum or AllowedTextValues field (at least every value of language and numerals), with B and A using the same value. Add 'g1.enumValuesAttacked' to the ratchet, and keep plant L1 as a gate self-test.
- Gap evidence: In a clone at 3b3b0b6, apply plant-L1-arabic-digits-leak.diff (UpdatePreferences: when request.Numerals == 'arab', read and write /tmp/erp-digits-note.txt and append the previous caller's e-mail to displayName). Run Erp.Gates.Tests with filter 'Process!=self-http&Process!=self-company&Process!=self-noninterference&Load!=Timing': G1HttpIsolationTests passes (13 m 17 s), and so do G1NonInterferenceTests, G1ProcessStateTests and G1TenantSourceTests (plants-L1-N1-gates.log; the only failure there is G2SubjectTests, from the separate N1 plant). ## 2026-10-07T02:05:19Z · p02-tenancy · round 4 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `9de44bff5ec50d34eb0f319fa9f57dcba2ce8c9f`
- Critic: fresh critic, p02-tenancy round 4
- Biggest gap: **Branch-scope rules on company-wide actions have no gate: a one-branch user who may rename or deactivate the company passes every gate, and the screens offer branch actions the server refuses** — Round 3's misses are fixed: P3, C3 and C4 are now caught by G1 and G2 gates, as are all six new variants I planted. One part of the branch layer is still guarded only by hand-written tests. The branch attack treats branch Z's own rows as the victim. It never asks whether a user limited to one branch can change something every branch shares. With the 'needs every branch' refusal removed from PUT /api/tenancy/companies/{id} (plant P7), an administrator limited to one warehouse can rename the legal entity, change its TRN and trade licence, or deactivate the whole company, and every gate stays green. Only one module test fails. For a UAE group whose branch managers are given the Administrator role inside their own branch, that means a branch manager could take the Dubai LLC's legal identity offline for everyone. On screen, the tenancy screen gate checks branch scope only on the company record and logo. The real build offers a one-branch administrator the company's Add-branch line, New on the Branches screen and an editable branch Code, and the server refuses all three. The fix: in the branch attack, add the company record (and anything ICompanyOwned that every branch shares) to what a branch-limited attacker must not change, with a valid write to PUT /companies/{X} and the logo endpoints expected to be refused. Also extend screens.test.tsx so that, with everyBranch false, the branch line, New branch and the branch code disappear, and plant-test both.
- Gap evidence: plants/P7.diff (one line in CompanyEndpoints.Update: the HoldsEveryBranch refusal made unreachable). plants/P7-gates.log: G1BranchScopeAttackTests, G1CompanyScopeTests (2) and G2CompanyAccessGrantTests all Passed. plants/P7-tenancy-module.log: only GrantRulesTests.A_branch_limited_administrator_sees_reads_changes_and_gives_only_their_own_branch Failed. To reproduce: python3 plants/apply.py <clone> P7 && dotnet build Erp.slnx -c Release && dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests --filter FullyQualifiedName~G1BranchScopeAttackTests passes. Screen: ui-branch.log and 07-one-branch-admin-branch-line.jpg. On ./erp up, as admin@alnoor.example, create a user with the Administrator role and PUT /api/tenancy/access/{id} with {companyId: ALN-DXB, allBranches: false, branchIds: [AQZ-WH]}. Sign in as that user and open the ALN-DXB company: 'branch line offered: 1', and Add answers 'Only someone who works in every branch of the company may create a branch or change a branch code.' The Branches screen offers New, and the branch form's Code is editable and refused on save.

Alt+C opens the switcher, which filters as you type. Arabic is RTL, with Arabic names in headers and the switcher. ## 2026-10-07T03:22:12Z · p05-list-search · round 4 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `fdd08eeabb223e0c5824a86da276ad4f1f0e1707`
- Critic: fresh critic, p05-list-search round 4
- Biggest gap: **The gates miss a tenant-count leak and a shared-view takeover planted in the list framework** — Every later module will register its lists with p05 and rely on G1 and G2 to attack them automatically. Two faults planted in p05's own code get past every gate. (1) G1: a process-wide memo kept in a static delegate field of ListBinding<T> passes both the process-state gate and the list-answer gate. The process-state gate skips statics of generic types as roots and counts Func fields as immutable. The list-answer gate never judges the total (or groups) on keyset continuation pages. The result is that one tenant scrolling to page 2 sees the other tenant's row count. (2) G2: a personal-route PUT/DELETE that also matches shared views lets a read-only user rename or delete the workspace's shared views without lists.views.share, and all 27 G2 tests pass. The G2 list test never sends a shared view's id to the personal routes, or a personal view's id to the shared routes. Fixes: (a) walk static fields of closed generic instantiations of product types, or reject delegate and closure fields unless reviewed, and walk delegate targets as ReachableState does elsewhere; (b) in ListAnswers, judge total and groups on every walked page, after the other tenant has walked the same query; (c) in G2, cross the ids: a reader sends the shared view's id to every personal route, and a sharer sends another user's personal view id to every shared route, and the workspace's views must stay unchanged.
- Gap evidence: Apply gauntlet/evidence/p05-list-search/r4/plants/L6-static-memo-next-page-total.patch and run `dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release --filter FullyQualifiedName~Erp.Gates.Tests.G1`: 38/38 pass (plants/run-L6-g1.log). Apply plants/P4-personal-route-writes-shared-views.patch and run the G2 filter: 27/27 pass (plants/run-P4-g2.log). Live on a stack with both plants (plants/plant-live-demo.txt, script scripts/plant-live.mjs): gulfsteel page 1 total 1004, alnoor page 1 total 3004, gulfsteel page 2 total 3004. viewer@alnoor gets PUT /api/lists/identity.users/views/{TeamViewId} 200 (renamed), then DELETE 204, while PUT /shared-views/{id} correctly gives 403.
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation (product as built): **PASS** — Gate suite green inside verify. My own attacks as admin@gulfsteel against alnoor (100,004 users from the shared dataset), in attack-api.txt, found 0 leaks: name, e-mail and filter searches for alnoor's needle, an OR-true filter, quote injection, % and _ wildcards, X-Tenant/X-Tenant-Id headers, tenant/tenantId query parameters, groupBy, alnoor's keyset cursor replayed, skip=99000, GET/PUT/DELETE on alnoor's shared and personal view ids (all 404, and both views were unchanged afterwards), and CSV/XLSX/PDF list exports. Database (db-inspect.txt): lists.saved_views has RLS enabled and forced with the standard policy, and erp_app has no BYPASSRLS. Unbound, erp_app sees 0 views and 0 users. Bound to gulfsteel it sees 0 alnoor rows, an INSERT for alnoor is refused by RLS, and an UPDATE of alnoor's rows touches 0 rows.
  - G1 catches tenant leaks planted in p05's surface: **FAIL** — Plant L6 (plants/L6-static-memo-next-page-total.patch) adds to ListBinding<T> a static readonly Func memo, a closure over a ConcurrentDictionary keyed by list\|search\|filter with no tenant. Continuation pages (after=) reuse the total that the last first page counted. All 38 G1 tests PASSED with it (plants/run-L6-g1.log, 16.6 min), process-state included (run-L6-processstate.log). Live on a plant stack (plants/plant-live-demo.txt): gulfsteel page 1 of search 'a' has total 1004, alnoor page 1 has total 3004, and gulfsteel page 2 then shows total 3004, which is alnoor's count. Two blind spots: (1) the process-state roots skip static fields of generic types (t.ContainsGenericParameters), and a delegate-typed field is judged immutable, so the captured dictionary is never reached; (2) ListAnswers judges only the first page's total and groups, never the totals of the keyset pages it walks. Gates that held: L7 (the same memo used for first-page totals) was caught by the list-answer check, with 102 wrong answers (run-L7-http.log), so r1's L3 gap stays fixed. L8 (a permissive SELECT policy on lists.saved_views) was caught by G1DatabaseIsolation (run-L8-db.log).
  - G2 permissions (product as built): **PASS** — Gate suite green. Live: the viewer gets list read access, no share checkbox and only Copy among the bulk actions. noaccess gets 'No access' on the screen and 403 from the API.
  - G2 catches unpermissioned actions planted in p05's surface: **FAIL** — Plant P4 (plants/P4-personal-route-writes-shared-views.patch): the personal routes PUT/DELETE /api/lists/{key}/views/{id} find a view when it is shared OR the caller's own. All 27 G2 tests PASSED (plants/run-P4-g2.log). Live (plant-live-demo.txt): viewer@alnoor does not hold lists.views.share and gets 403 on PUT /shared-views/{id}. The same user gets 200 on PUT /views/{sharedId}, which renames the workspace's Team view, and 204 on DELETE /views/{sharedId}, which deletes it. Only Erp.Modules.Lists.Tests caught it (1 failure, run-P4-lists-module-tests.log), not the gate. The G2 list test sends only the administrator's personal-view id to personal routes and never a shared-view id. Gates that held: P2b (shared DELETE with the list-permission filter and handler check removed) and P3 (personal PUT/DELETE matched by id alone) were each caught by G2 (run-P2b…, run-P3…), so r1's P2 gap and p03's P3 route stay fixed.
  - G3 clean clone: **PASS** — Fresh clone at fdd08ee. The ratchet check passed. ./erp up with ERP_SEED_USERS_CSV gave a running demo with 100,004 users in 93 s. The port and project overrides worked.

Saved-view insert and delete, and the user bulk deactivation, write audit.entries rows with the actor and old/new values (checked in the database). Trigram GIN indexes exist on display_name/email_normalized and display_name_ar, plus (tenant_id, column, id) btrees.

- Clean clone to running demo: PASS in 1669s — ./erp verify on the fresh clone passed: 417 .NET, 262 web unit, 64 e2e and 146 compare tests, and the ratchet passed (verify.log). The run took 1669 s from 'slot taken'. ./erp up served the seeded demo with the shared dataset in 93 s (up.log).
- Odoo side by side:

  | Task | Ours steps / keys / s / human s | Odoo steps / keys / s / human s | Winner | Notes |

  | find-user (find one user among 100,000; p16 contacts not built, so the largest list, the dataset's 100,000 users) | 3 / 15 / 0.513 / 8.6 | 6 / 20 / 2.371 / 13.2 | ours | Medians of 3 verified runs per product, branding hidden (compare/). The committed ours driver is not the shortest path: it clicks into a search box that already has the focus, and it loses on human seconds (14.35 vs 13.2; compare-committed-driver/). I wrote critic drivers (scripts/find-user.ours.critic.mjs and find-user.odoo.critic.mjs). Ours has variants enter, row, prefixes and palette. 'prefixes' types the first three letters of each word ('Maj Ani Pil') and Enter opens the best-ranked match, which is verified to be the user. Odoo gained a 'menus-suggestion' variant, which clicks the search suggestion instead of pressing Enter: 20 keys, down from its baseline 21. With the full name typed in both products, keystrokes tie at 20 and ours loses. Ours wins only through word-prefix relevance ranking, a real product capability. |

- Other findings:
  - Money in the list framework has no currency. ListColumnType.Money is a bare decimal with no currency, rate or base-amount link (ListColumn has no currency field). GroupsAsync sums aggregate Money columns across rows whatever their currency, so the first multi-currency list (invoices in AED and USD) will show group totals that add different currencies (ListBinding.cs ~line 764-790). Rule 2 needs a money column bound to amount plus currency, with totals per currency or in base currency.
  - Bulk actions that change data only work on chosen rows. With 'All 3,265 matching rows selected', Activate/Deactivate are disabled ('Acts on chosen rows only'); only Copy works on everything that matches (11-bulk-all-matching.jpg, ui5b.txt). Odoo can archive every matching record after 'select all'.
  - Fixed since r1, and must stay fixed: header sort and column menus work by keyboard (Enter/Space sort, the menu opens with arrows; ui2.txt). Columns line up at 1440 px (identical cell x on every row). An OR filter can be built ('match any'). Arabic spelling variants match ('ماجد انيل بيلاى' finds 'ماجد أنيل بيلاي'). Relevance ranking works and Enter opens the best match. Ctrl+A twice selects all that match. The users bulk actions Activate/Deactivate exist and are audited.
  - Cosmetic: in rows with an empty Roles cell, the row separator in the Roles column sits a few pixels off the rest of the row (01-users-list-en.jpg).
  - Performance over 100,004 users (5-run medians, round trip): exact name 28 ms, 'pillai' 28 ms, Arabic two words 144 ms, search 'a' 107 ms, filter 40 ms, groupBy 69 ms, skip 50,000 105 ms. A three-word search ran 124-317 ms. End on 100,004 rows fills the last row in 114 ms; 50 PageUps settle in under 1 s. Only 33 DOM rows are rendered.
  - Keyboard and screens: the search box has the focus on arrival; ArrowDown enters the grid; Enter opens the record panel; Esc returns; Space and Shift+Arrow select. Saved views (personal, shared, default) persist and open by default. The viewer gets no share option. Arabic is RTL with Arabic chrome and plural counts ('100,004 مستخدمين', 'مستخدم واحد').
  - [route: p03-identity] The committed ours driver for find-user clicks into the already-focused search box (an extra step and hand move) and does not use Enter-opens-best-match. As committed, it loses human seconds to Odoo (14.35 vs 13.2). It should adopt the shorter variants (scripts/find-user.ours.critic.mjs).
  - [route: p01-odoo-rig] The Odoo find-user baseline misses a shorter keystroke path: clicking the search suggestion instead of pressing Enter verifies with 20 keystrokes, against the baseline's 21 (scripts/find-user.odoo.critic.mjs, 'menus-suggestion').
  - [route: p00-foundation] The process-state inspector (G1ProcessStateTests.Roots) skips every type with ContainsGenericParameters and treats every Delegate-typed field as immutable, so any generic product class can hold cross-tenant state in a static Func/closure without being seen. This weakness applies beyond lists.
  - The verify slot script defaults to 2 slots (ERP_VERIFY_SLOTS); this critic waited 5025 s for one.

## 2026-10-07T06:22:24Z · p06-form-report · round 2 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `889384f3e335ae9960e926ed27189053134cc10f`
- Critic: fresh critic, p06-form-report round 2
- Biggest gap: **The screen permission gate does not check keyboard actions: Ctrl+S on a read-only record can send a save and every gate passes** — The product is keyboard-first, so its shortcuts are its main controls. The form kernel (web/src/kernel/forms) decides who may save twice: the `forms.save`/`forms.saveEnter` shortcuts are enabled only when the form is editable, and useRecordForm.save() returns early without canEdit. If both checks are lost, a read-only user's Ctrl+S sends PUT. All 268 web unit tests still pass, as do the 22 screen plant self-tests and the e2e suite. The existing test 'shows a record read-only … no save' presses Ctrl+S but asserts nothing afterwards. The gate should press every registered form and list chord (save, save-enter, discard, new, delete and so on) on a screen whose user lacks the permission, assert that no request other than GET leaves, and be plant-tested against this fault. Every server-side plant this round was caught, including round 1's L2/P1/P2 misses. Separately, and most visible to users, CSV/XLSX exports stop silently at 20,000 rows (see other findings).
- Gap evidence: plants/web-plants.sh, block W2: in RecordForm.tsx `enabled: editable` becomes `enabled: true` on forms.save, and in useRecordForm.ts `if (!current.canEdit \|\| busy)` becomes `if (busy)`. Result: vitest 268/268, tenancy-plant-self-test 17/17 caught, identity 5/5 caught (plants/web-plants.log). plants/web-w2-probe.sh adds `expect(calls.filter(c => c.method !== 'GET')).toEqual([])` after the Ctrl+S in forms.test.tsx:170. It passes unplanted, and with W2 it fails with 'expected [ PUT /api/things/t1 ] to deeply equal []' (plants/web-w2-probe.log).
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation (product as built): **PASS** — Gate suites pass inside verify (suite plus self-http, self-company and self-noninterference). Then gulfsteel's admin requested the same URLs three times each: plain, with X-Tenant-Id, and with X-Tenant plus X-Company-Id. All 462 answers (348×200, 96×404, 18×400) were searched after PDF text extraction and XLSX unzip: 0 contained alnoor markers (names, codes, TRNs, ids). Database: erp_app has no super and no bypassrls, cannot SET ROLE erp_owner or disable RLS, and sees only the bound tenant's rows. Every tenant table has RLS enabled and forced. The tenant binding is tied to the transaction timestamp.

With W2 applied, all 268 web unit tests pass, as do the tenancy (17) and identity (5) screen plant self-tests (plants/web-plants.log), and no e2e spec presses Ctrl+S on a read-only form. ## 2026-10-07T19:54:55Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 6 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `b9919e61efb4592c00831356ad76642acd8dd069`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 6
- Biggest gap: **A driver can still act uncounted or finish off the clock through the page: async continuations escape the sentinel, and the after-clock freeze does not stop late answers** — Round 6 closed the Node side (the sandboxed driver process). The browser side is still open in two ways.  (1) Uncounted actions. The sentinel only refuses what the condition does synchronously. Anything it schedules runs after disarm() puts every refused method back: a native `await` (which never calls the patched Promise.prototype.then), `Element.animate().onfinish`, and similar callbacks. A ctx.read or op.waitFor condition can therefore fill fields and click Save with no step counted. The MutationObserver, focus and address checks look only at the synchronous window.  (2) Wrong timer. Emulation.setScriptExecutionDisabled does not stop the page's in-flight fetch continuations. A driver can return from run() before the product answers, and verify() can read the page in short ctx.reads until the answer is on screen. The meter misses this: - page reads are not 'repeated back-end reads'; - each pause stays under 1 s; - the slower-first-pass rule needs a gap over 3 s.  The harness's own T2b test waits only 0.9 s on a 2 s answer, so it cannot see the freeze fail. Removing freezePages altogether leaves the suite green (mutation M5).  Possible fixes: - Run conditions in an isolated world (Playwright's utility world or a CDP isolated context) so page globals and the product's handlers are out of reach. - Or refuse any condition whose source contains async, await, function scheduling or observer constructors, and keep the sentinel armed until the next turn of the page's event loop (check for DOM mutations and requests started after the condition returns, before the next counted step). - Treat any non-GET request, or any DOM change, that starts while no counted step is in progress as an uncounted action. - Make the freeze real: pause the page with Debugger.pause, or abort in-flight responses at the clock and snapshot the DOM there. - Make verify() read a DOM snapshot taken at finish(), not the live page. - Add S1, S2 and T3 as self-tests. Also test that the frozen screen stays unchanged after more than the product's answer time.

## 2026-10-08T02:36:30Z · p02-tenancy · round 6 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `c25bb61a576a49ba4ce90efc80bd62290ab3999f`
- Critic: fresh critic, p02-tenancy round 6
- Biggest gap: **The branch attack only tries branch Z's own ids, never the attacker's own company: branch-owned rows reached through a company-level read or export pass every gate** — The branch layer is enforced only in the application (the EF branch filter), so every read that selects by company must apply it. The branch attack (CompanyAttack.RunAsync, Layer.Branch) does two things. It sends branch Z's ids and texts into routes, queries and bodies. It also checks company X's shared records for writes. It never makes the branch-limited attacker read company X's own reports, exports and per-user screens with valid company-X parameters (or with none) and then look for branch Z's markers in the answer. Three one-line plants that skip the branch filter passed every G1 and G2 gate, and each is a real leak on a running build. B3: the company profile report (CSV, PDF, JSON) prints every branch of company X with its phone. B3b: a plain GET of the branch directory report lists every branch. B1: GET /api/tenancy/access/{userId} offers every branch's code and name as options. Only the hand-written module test GrantRulesTests noticed B1. This is the same pattern that failed rounds 3 and 4, a branch rule guarded only by module tests. Every later module's 'by company' report, export or job would inherit the blind spot. For a UAE group that limits a warehouse keeper or branch manager to one branch, that means branch-confidential data (other branches' contacts, and later their stock, sales and costs) can leak through any company-level export with no gate turning red. The fix: in the branch layer, also call every GET (reports and list prints included) as the branch-limited attacker with only valid own parameters. That means no parameters, company=X, and the company-X filters in the list contract. Judge each answer against branch Z's markers, the code and names stored when branch Z was created, so the 'values the attacker sent or stored' exclusion cannot hide them. Then plant-test B1, B3 and B3b as gate self-tests.
- Gap evidence: Plants: plants-B1-B3-B3b.diff, plant-B1.diff and plant-B3b.diff (each one line adding IgnoreQueryFilters([TenancyDbContext.BranchFilterName])). With B3: G1BranchScopeAttackTests, G1HttpIsolationTests, G2ReportDataTests and G1CompanyScopeTests all Passed (plants-batchX-gates.log). With B1 alone and with B3b alone, G1BranchScopeAttackTests Passed (plant-B1-gates.log, plant-B3b-gates.log), and with B3b, G2ReportDataTests and G1HttpIsolationTests Passed too (plants-batchY-gates.log). To reproduce: apply plant-B3b.diff to c25bb61, run dotnet build Erp.slnx -c Release, then dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release --no-build --filter FullyQualifiedName~G1BranchScopeAttackTests; the gate passes. Real leak: run ERP_PROJECT=x ERP_HTTP_PORT=20270 ERP_DB_PORT=20271 ./erp up with the plant applied. As admin@alnoor.example, create an Administrator user and PUT /api/tenancy/access/{id} {companies:[{companyId: ALN-DXB, allBranches:false, branchIds:[AQZ-WH]}]}. Signed in as that user, GET /api/tenancy/branches lists only AQZ-WH, while GET /api/reports/run/tenancy.branchDirectory?format=csv and /api/reports/run/tenancy.companyProfile?company=<ALN-DXB>&format=csv list DEIRA-HQ and DIP-SR as well (attack/b1b3b-plant.log, attack/b3-plant.log; the clean demo shows only AQZ-WH: attack/b1b3b-demo.log, attack/b3-demo.log).
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation: suite and hand attacks on the product as built: **PASS** — Every G1 test passed in ./erp verify (verify.log). Hand attack (attack/attack.mjs, attack/attack.log): signed in as admin@alnoor.example, I sent 48 requests at gulfsteel. They covered GET, PUT and DELETE on B's company, logo and branch, a POST of a branch into B's company, and workplace switches to B's company and branch. They also covered grants of B's company and branch to A's viewer, access to B's admin, list filters and searches on B's ids, and B's codes. Reports and exports were included: companyProfile and branchDirectory with company=B in json, csv, xlsx and pdf, plus list prints. Five tenant or company switch headers and a Host header were tried too. Every request got 404, 400 or an empty list. B's company and branch stayed at version 773 before and after. The one marker 'hit' was my own code GSF-RAK echoed back by a 201 in tenant A (codes are per tenant, so it is no oracle). Database (db-inspection.txt, db-attack.txt): RLS is enabled and forced on all 7 tenancy tables. The company_scope policy is RESTRICTIVE. erp_app is not a superuser and has no BYPASSRLS. As erp_app with no tenant bound it sees 0 companies and 0 tenants. A tenant GUC without the transaction marker sees 0 rows, and so does company_scope=all. An UPDATE on B's company changed 0 rows. DISABLE RLS was refused (not owner), and SET ROLE to erp_owner or postgres was denied.

## 2026-10-08T07:22:38Z · p05-list-search · round 5 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `0114d0528eb97a2d05cefff34c4d38649c63b3dc`
- Critic: fresh critic, p05-list-search round 5
- Biggest gap: **Offset (skip) pages are judged by no gate, so a tenant-blind total memo in the list engine passes all of G1** — Every later module registers its lists with p05 and relies on G1 to attack them automatically. The list engine has two paging modes, keyset (after=) and offset (skip=), and the client itself sends skip= when a user jumps through a long list. ListAnswers judges first pages and keyset pages only. NonInterference never pages with skip. A skip page whose total comes from state shared across tenants is therefore unseen whenever that state lives anywhere the structural gates miss. My plant L10 keeps it in a pooled scratch object: ObjectPool<ListPageScratch>, a DI singleton whose implementation is the framework's DefaultObjectPool<T>. The process-state gate only walks singletons whose implementation type is a product type, so it never reaches the object. The tenant-source scanner has no rule for object pools. Fixes: (a) in ListAnswers (and NonInterference), walk each query by offset as well: skip pages in lock step with the other tenant, with the total and groups of every skip page judged against the walked rows; (b) in process state, walk every singleton instance, framework implementation types included, down to the product objects it holds, or flag pools, caches and any singleton whose generic arguments are product types; (c) add object pools and IMemoryCache-like framework stores to the tenant-source 'process-global' rule.
- Gap evidence: Apply gauntlet/evidence/p05-list-search/r5/plants/L10-pooled-scratch-skip-total.patch. Then `dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release --filter FullyQualifiedName~Erp.Gates.Tests.G1` passes 38/38 (plants/run-L10-g1-all.log). Live on a stack with the plant (scripts/plant-live.mjs, plants/plant-live-L10.txt): GET /api/identity/users?take=50&search=a answers total 1004 to admin@gulfsteel and 3004 to admin@alnoor. Then gulfsteel's GET ...&skip=50 answers total 3004, which is alnoor's count. The same happens for search=khalid (28 vs 70) and filter language eq 'ar' (203 vs 640).

The bulk action POST /api/identity/users/matching/active gives no count oracle (its 409 reports gulfsteel's own counts only) and alnoor's needle stayed active. CSV/XLSX/PDF list exports were clean. GET/PUT/DELETE on alnoor's shared and personal view ids, through both routes, all answered 404, and both views were unchanged. ListBinding.QueryAsync takes a per-query scratch object from an ObjectPool<ListPageScratch>, registered as a DI singleton in ListsModule. The scratch keeps a total keyed by list\|search\|filter with no tenant and is never cleared, and skip pages reuse it. All 38 G1 tests passed (plants/run-L10-g1-all.log, 2005 s), as did ProcessState, TenantSource and NonInterference (run-L10-structural.log). (2) Process-state roots are product singletons and statics, and a singleton whose implementation type is a framework type (DefaultObjectPool<T>) holding product objects is never walked. ## 2026-10-08T08:21:04Z · p00-foundation · round 7 · WIN

- Commit judged: `bb96d2203dc7355eb1060aaabfd11a768ea28a25`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 7
- Biggest gap: **The sign-in win exists only on the team sign-in address; from a plain or per-user bookmark ours ties Odoo** — Ours beats Odoo on sign-in only when the user starts from the team address /?domain=<team domain>. There the screen moves to the password by itself once a whole e-mail in that domain is typed, which saves one step and one key (3/56/18.78 s against 4/57/19.06 s), a thin margin of 0.28 modelled seconds. From the plain address '/', a typed whole e-mail does not move on: the user presses Enter, giving 4 steps / 57 keys, the same as Odoo's e-mail, Tab, password, Enter. A personal bookmark carrying the e-mail is a tie as well: ours /?email=<e-mail> and Odoo /web/login?db=..&login=<e-mail> both pre-fill the e-mail and focus the password, so each takes password then Enter (2 steps, 21 keystrokes). In every start except the team address, ours wins only on machine seconds. To win on every start, the plain sign-in screen needs a path Odoo lacks. Options: move on to the password when the typed e-mail matches a known workspace domain, offer passkey or one-tap sign-in as the first path, or remember the last e-mail on the device even after an explicit sign-out (as a choice the user makes).
- Gap evidence: compare/results/*.json: in variant 'new-device-whole-e-mail' (start ?domain=demo-trading.example) ours scored 3 steps / 56 keys. attack/ours-email.mjs shows that after typing viewer@alnoor.example on plain '/', focus stays on the e-mail field ('ours plain / after whole e-mail focused: email'), and that ours /?email=viewer%40alnoor.example pre-fills the e-mail and focuses the password. attack/odoo-login.mjs shows Odoo /web/login?db=reference&login=signin.tester%40demo-trading.example does the same ('focused: password'). Both outputs are in browser-notes.txt. To reproduce, run COMPARE_OURS_URL=http://localhost:<port> node gauntlet/compare/run.mjs --task sign-in --product both.

## 2026-10-08T10:24:38Z · p03-identity · round 5 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `810b289fdb0042c7e123701871b50018c26d4c65`
- Critic: fresh critic, p03-identity round 5
- Biggest gap: **'All that match' deactivation ignores roles held per company: a helpdesk clerk can lock out a company manager, and no gate looks at that endpoint** — POST /api/identity/users/matching/active (UserBulkEndpoints.SetMatchingActive) decides who is 'stronger' only from workspace-wide identity.user_roles:   strongRoles = roles granting something caller.Has() lacks; exclude users with a user_roles row in strongRoles It never reads identity.user_company_roles, and it never refuses users whose roles lie in companies the caller cannot see (users.company_role_count greater than the visible count). The single-user rule, UserAccess.RefusalAsync, checks both.  This matters because roles per company are the UAE model the piece just added: 'accountant in the Dubai LLC, manager in the JAFZA FZE'. Once they are in use, every company-level manager can be deactivated and signed out by anyone holding identity.users.update, through the users list's 'select all that match'. That is a whole-workforce denial-of-service and a step in a takeover. Odoo's group-based access has no such hole.  The gates miss it. G2AccountTakeoverTests inventories only routes under /api/identity/users/{ and /roles/{, so an action that picks its targets by a search body is never aimed at a stronger user. Planting P2, the bulk stronger-user check removed entirely, also passed every gate.  Fix: - Make the bulk path apply the same rule as RefusalAsync, set-based. Exclude users with a user_company_roles row whose role grants something the caller lacks in that company. Exclude users whose company_role_count exceeds what the caller can see. - Extend the takeover and grant gates to every endpoint that changes user or role rows, including body-selected ones like matching/active. Aim them at a target whose only grants are per company, and at one with roles in a company the caller does not work in. - Add P2 and R1 as gate self-tests.
- Gap evidence: Live, on a demo from a clean clone of 810b289 (attacks/G2-bulk-deactivate-company-manager.txt; script attacks/g2bulk.sh): 1. As admin@alnoor, create role 'Helpdesk clerk' (identity.users.read, identity.users.update) and user clerk@alnoor with it. 2. Create 'Dubai Manager' with roleIds [] and companyRoles [{Company manager, ALN-DXB}]. 3. As the clerk, PUT /api/identity/users/{manager} with isActive=false: 403 identity.userBeyondOwnCompanies. POST …/sessions/revoke: 403. 4. As the clerk, POST /api/identity/users/matching/active with {active:false, search:'dubai.manager@alnoor.example', filter:'', expectedCount:1}: 200 {matched:1, changed:1, refusedBeyondOwn:0}. 5. The manager's live session now gets 401, and their next sign-in fails.  In the test harness: copy plants/CriticR5Proof.cs into tests/Erp.Modules.Identity.Tests and run dotnet test --filter FullyQualifiedName~CriticR5. R1 fails on the shipped product, and P1–P3 pass (plants/unplanted-proofs/proofs.log).  Gate blindness: apply plants/P1-P3-permissions.diff. No gate failure names matching/active (plants/run2/gates.log); P1 and P3 were caught. The unplanted verify passes all 1,053 tests with R1 present (verify.log).

Every call by id answered 404 or 400, and B's records read back unchanged (attacks/attack-a-vs-b.txt). Eleven differential checks compared B's role and company ids with random ids in create and edit bodies (roleIds, companyRoles, default company, workplace), in reports and in a filter. All gave identical answers. A role name or e-mail that B uses answered 201, the same as an unused one (attacks/attack-a-vs-b-differential.txt). The 'leaks' flagged in attack-a-vs-b.txt are my own tenant A user with the address admin@gulfsteel.example, which tenant A may legitimately hold. As erp_app: no tenant bound gives 0 rows. Bound to A, 0 rows of other tenants and UPDATE 0. An insert into B's user_company_roles was refused by RLS. A session-level tenant was ignored. DISABLE RLS, SET ROLE erp_owner and row_security=off were all refused (attacks/db-app-role.txt).

## 2026-10-08T17:24:42Z · p06-form-report · round 3 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `cb26098035ad0d88d695704da77150914c3d44a2`
- Critic: fresh critic, p06-form-report round 3
- Biggest gap: **The permission gate never reads exports or list printouts: role data withheld from a user still goes out in their CSV and printed lists, and every gate passes** — For a UAE trading company, exports and printouts are how data leaves the system: Excel to the accountant, PDFs to auditors. This piece owns that surface. The gate passes any export or printout that contains columns or record names the caller's roles do not grant. G2ReportData judges reports only as format=json, so a CSV/XLSX export that ignores the withheld columns (P6) passes. No gate judges what /api/reports/lists/<key> prints, so a list print that names another area's records without checking the caller may read them (P8) passes. Round 2 fixed the server plants of rounds 1 and 2, but only for JSON and report runs. The gate should run every report and every printable list in every format (json, pdf after text extraction, csv, xlsx) and both languages, as users holding exactly the report's or list's permission (and each extra read permission). Every value printed, record names in ValuesFrom columns included, must also be shown by an endpoint those permissions open. The gate should be plant-tested with P6 and P8.
- Gap evidence: plants/plant-P6-exports-ignore-withheld-columns.patch (ReportEndpoints.RunAsync: Withheld = empty for csv/xlsx) and plants/plant-P8-list-print-names-without-permission.patch (ReportEndpoints.NamesAsync: the caller.Has(source.List.Permission) check removed), applied to cb26098. I ran tests/Erp.Gates.Tests in the toolbox image (plants/run-gates.sh, filter Load!=Timing without the self-test processes). - Commit judged: `462c349da3f7685758ddaebfc394d51963059e76`
- Critic: fresh critic, p03-identity round 6
- Biggest gap: **The new 'all that match' takeover gate never uses a caller whose own grants come from a company role, so a fault that lets a grant held in one company cover every company passes every gate** — The product now applies the per-company rule correctly: r5's R1 is fixed, and all live and harness checks hold. The gate added for it is half-blind, though. SetTakeover (tests/Erp.Gates.Tests/G2/SetTakeover.cs) gives each caller a workspace-wide role and aims at targets whose roles are held everywhere, in one company, or in a company the caller does not work in. It never builds a caller whose own permissions come from a company role. So UserBulkEndpoints.BeyondCallerAsync's coveredThere ('role/company': the caller's grant counts only in the company where it is held) is never exercised. Planting Pc, which keys coveredThere by role alone, lets a clerk who manages users in the Dubai LLC deactivate and sign out a manager of the JAFZA FZE. That shape is exactly the per-company model UAE groups use, and Pc passed all 484 .NET tests and every gate. On the one-user path the same fault (r5's P1) is caught by G2CompanyRoleTests; the set-based path has no equivalent.  Fix: - Extend SetTakeover, and the one-user takeover inventory, with callers whose endpoint permission and target-covering grants are held through a role in company X. They should work in X and Y and be aimed at a user holding the same grants in Y; that user must stay unchanged. - Keep a control: the same grants held in X are changed. - Add Pc as a LeakyModule self-test, like bugs 53 and 54.  Also still open, and routed to p00 as in r5: the G1 body attack and write oracle never place tenant B's ids inside arrays of objects. r5's L3, companyRoles[].companyId, is still missed.

  - [route: p00-foundation] ./erp verify's processor-time ceiling (verify.cpuSeconds 9,000) depends on load. The same commit used 10,345 s at load 39 to 105 and failed the ratchet, then 7,900 s at load 8 to 16 and passed. With two verify slots this machine is rarely quiet, so a newcomer's one command can fail with every test green. The wall-time ceiling is already exempt on a busy machine; the processor-time one is not.
  - [route: p00-foundation] Row versions are global PostgreSQL transaction ids (xmin-like): tenant B's role got version 944, and tenant A's next create got 945. Tenant A can therefore count other tenants' write activity between its own writes. It is a low-grade side channel, but it is cross-tenant information (g1-rolename-followup.txt).
  - [route: p00-foundation] The G1 write oracle and HTTP body attack still never put tenant B's ids inside arrays of objects. r5's L3 (companyRoles[].companyId: identityNotTheirCompany for B's company, unknownIds for an id that exists nowhere) went unflagged in plants/run1/gates.log. Line-item modules (invoice lines, order lines) will inherit this blind spot.
  - Unchanged since r1: behind the default compose deployment every sign-in attempt is recorded from the gateway 172.22.0.1, and nine failures paused that source for the account (attacks/lockout.txt). ERP_KNOWN_PROXIES and ERP_KNOWN_NETWORKS now exist, but the default setup still records only the gateway. Unblock works (204), and reset password shows a set-up code with its expiry.
  - The user_company_roles RLS policy lets a session read and write its own rows in any company (company_allowed OR user_id = actor), and with no separate WITH CHECK this applies to INSERT too. The app refuses self-assignment (cannotChangeOwnAccess, or unknownIds for unseen companies), so it is not exploitable through the API. Still, the database layer would let erp_app add a company role for the acting user in any company. Consider a WITH CHECK that drops the own-row branch.

## 2026-10-09T00:32:27Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 8 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `59e3533696ddb09a70c170c46a6e02711857f25f`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 8
- Biggest gap: **Set-up can do the whole task off the clock: the 'already done before the clock' check is defeated because verify() can tell the before-clock call apart, and a run with zero counted steps is accepted** — Set-up may act on the product (fixtures). The only guard against set-up doing the task itself is a single verify() call before the clock, and a run counts as 'already done' only when that call returns verified === true. But the driver process calls verify(ctx, undefined) before the clock and verify(ctx, outcome) after it. A driver therefore knows which call it is in. It can answer 'not done' (or throw, which the runner reads as 'not done') before the clock, and read the real end state after it. Nothing requires run() to take any counted step. So the task can be done uncounted through the product's API in set-up: 0 steps, 0 keys, a few milliseconds, and the comparison records a win over any honest path. The lint, the baselines check, the health check and all 315 harness tests stay green. This turns any lost task into a recorded win and makes every ours number untrustworthy until it is closed.  Possible fixes: - Give verify() the same arguments before and after the clock, so it cannot tell the calls apart: no outcome at all, or a recorded outcome replayed. - Better, judge the end state with the harness's own reads: snapshot the back-end reads verify() makes before set-up, at the start and after the clock, and require the task's end state to be absent at the start. - Refuse a screen task with zero counted steps. - Record set-up's back-end writes, and refuse a run whose set-up wrote to a resource verify() later reads, other than to restore a start state. - Add P1, P1b and P2 as self-tests.  Also close the three mutations the self-tests miss: chord keystrokes (A1), the reference's best path per metric (A16) and a scroll counted as a step (A26).

## 2026-10-09T00:53:00Z · p05-list-search · round 7 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `f1ce287ac83390e1d2ee6f07603511cc371d6434`
- Critic: fresh critic, p05-list-search round 7
- Biggest gap: **Sorted list queries are judged by no gate, and file-local classes are invisible to process state, so a tenant-blind total memo in the list engine passes all of G1** — Every later module registers its lists with p05 and relies on G1 to attack them automatically. The list contract has five query inputs: search, filter, sort, paging and grouping. ListAnswers, the only gate that judges what a total adds up to, sends search, filter and groupBy but never sort. Every header click in the client sends sort. HttpIsolation and ListContract send sort, but never with a search and never judging the total. NonInterference cannot see static state by design and leaves it to the process-state gate. That gate's IsGenerated skips any type whose name contains '<'. That includes every C# `file`-local class, which is an ordinary language feature, so the statics of such a class are neither inspected nor walked. Plant L11 combines the two: a `file static class` in ListBinding.cs keeps a ConcurrentDictionary of totals keyed by list\|search\|filter\|sort with no tenant. Fixes: (a) in ListAnswers (and NonInterference's list requests), walk every query also with each sortable column in both directions, alone and combined with the searches and filters already used, and judge the total and groups of every page; (b) in ProcessState/ReachableState, treat as generated only types with [CompilerGenerated] or [GeneratedCode] whose names match closure, display-class or state-machine patterns, and inspect C# file-local types (<File>F…__Name) like any other type; (c) add a tenant-source or process-state rule for static mutable collections (ConcurrentDictionary, Dictionary and the like) in any product type.
- Gap evidence: Apply gauntlet/evidence/p05-list-search/r7/plants/L11-file-local-static-sorted-search-total.patch and run `dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release --filter FullyQualifiedName~Erp.Gates.Tests.G1`: 53/53 pass (plants/run-L11-g1-all.log; ProcessState and TenantSource alone in plants/run-L11-structural.log). Live on a stack with the plant (scripts/live-probe.mjs <url> l11, plants/plant-live-L11.txt): after admin@alnoor asks GET /api/identity/users?take=50&search=khalid&sort=displayName (total 52), admin@gulfsteel gets total 52 for the same request, but only 28 rows; without sort it says 28. On screen, gulfsteel sorts Users by Name, types 'khalid' and sees '52 users' (plants/plant-L11-gulfsteel-sorted-search-shows-other-tenant-total.jpg).
- Hard gates:

Alnoor's needle stayed active. Control on the real product (control-L11-real-product.txt): sorted searches give each tenant its own total. Database (db-inspect.txt): lists.saved_views has RLS enabled and forced with tenant_isolation. erp_owner, erp_app and erp_auth have neither superuser nor BYPASSRLS. Unbound, erp_app sees 0 users and 0 views. Bound to gulfsteel, it sees 0 alnoor users and 0 views, and an UPDATE of alnoor's views touches 0 rows.
  - G1 catches tenant leaks planted in p05's surface: **FAIL** — Plant L11 (plants/L11-file-local-static-sorted-search-total.patch, 13 lines in ListBinding.cs) is a C# file-local class (`file static class SortedSearchTotals`) holding a static ConcurrentDictionary<string,int>. A request with both an explicit sort and a search reuses the total first counted for list\|search\|filter\|sort, whatever the tenant. All 53 G1 tests PASSED with it (plants/run-L11-g1-all.log, 32 min), including ProcessState, TenantSource, NonInterference, HttpIsolation/ListAnswers and WriteOracle. Live on a plant stack (plants/plant-live-L11.txt): gulfsteel's GET /api/identity/users?search=khalid&sort=displayName answers total 52, which is alnoor's count; gulfsteel has 28 rows and the same query without sort says 28. The same happens for search=a (2005 vs 1005) and search=a with filter language eq 'ar' (421 vs 203). The users screen shows it: sort by Name, type 'khalid', and gulfsteel sees '52 users' (plants/plant-L11-gulfsteel-sorted-search-shows-other-tenant-total.jpg). Blind spots: (1) ListAnswers never sends sort=. Its queries are search, filter and groupBy only, while the client sends sort whenever a user clicks a header. HttpIsolation and ListContract send sort without a search, and NonInterference cannot see statics by design. (2) ProcessState.IsGenerated skips every type whose metadata name contains '<'. Odoo (odoo-shortest-*.txt) searches name, login and e-mail with ilike and orders by name. No 4-letter fragment of the name puts the user in its first 40 rows. The best is 'il pi' (23 rows, the user is row 13), then a click on the search suggestion and a click on the row: 5 keystrokes. I added that as Odoo variant 'critic-shortest-suggestion' (scripts/find-user.odoo.critic.mjs). I added that as ours variant 'critic-row' (scripts/find-user.ours.critic.mjs). Result: ours wins on every metric through 'critic-row' only. Every committed ours path loses, on keystrokes, to Odoo's critic path: 'shortest' 8, 'prefixes' 15, 'enter' 21, against 5. The margins are thin: 1 keystroke and 0.13 s of machine time. No metric was left out (none tied at zero). I topped up the shared rig's job runs with tools/odoo-reference/up.sh first (no restart; job_runs had fallen below 100,000). |

## 2026-10-09T14:32:28Z · p02-tenancy · round 8 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `d98540a0e80d46023cfcbfe6f5f62b6a1d0f9e12`
- Critic: fresh critic, p02-tenancy round 8
- Biggest gap: **No gate aims a one-company administrator at a user who also works in another company: the default-company scope check in UserWorkplaces can be deleted and every gate stays green** — The new IUserWorkplaces service (src/Modules/Tenancy/Erp.Modules.Tenancy/Workplace/UserWorkplaces.cs) is the only thing that stops an administrator limited to company X from changing the default working company of a user who works in X and Y. Identity's target check (UserAccess.RefusalAsync) only counts hidden company roles, and a user whose role is workspace-wide has none. With the 4-line 'if (elsewhere) return UserBeyondScope' removed, an ALN-SHJ-only administrator rewrites the default company of a Read-only user who works in ALN-DXB and ALN-SHJ, and the server answers 200. Yet G1CompanyIsolationTests, both G2CompanyRoleTests and all six G2AccountTakeoverTests still pass. The gates' company callers (G2/CompanyCallers.cs) always work in both X and Y, and the company attack never targets a user whose access reaches beyond the attacker's companies. This is the same 'records more than one company shares' blind spot round 6 found for the workspace record, now one level down, on users. Every later module that writes something about a user (employee, salesperson or approver settings) would inherit it. For a UAE group where each entity has its own administrator, that means one entity's admin could quietly change people who also work for its sister companies. The fix is to add, to the company layer, targets that hold access in X and in Y, with workspace-wide roles and with company roles. Every user-addressed write (default company, access, profile) aimed at them by an X-only administrator must be refused. Then plant-test PU as a gate self-test.
- Gap evidence: plants/plant-PU.diff (UserWorkplaces.SetAsync without the elsewhere check). Gate run with it in place: dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests -c Release --no-build --filter 'FullyQualifiedName~G1CompanyIsolationTests\|FullyQualifiedName~G2CompanyRoleTests\|FullyQualifiedName~G2AccountTakeoverTests'. All passed (plants/plant-Z-gates.log; the only failures there are the two DB tests on the separate L3 plant). Real fault: build the plant, run ERP_PROJECT=x ERP_HTTP_PORT=21270 ERP_DB_PORT=21271 ERP_SEED_VOLUME=1000 ./erp up, then node attack/pu.mjs http://localhost:21270. Plant: 'CL PUT U2 default-company = ALN-SHJ: 200' and 'U2 default after: ALN-SHJ' (attack/pu-plant.log). Clean build, same request: 403 identity.userBeyondOwnCompanies (attack/probe-cl.log).

- Biggest gap: **The Companies screen offers New and an editable company Code to administrators who do not work in every company, the server refuses both, and the screen gate never checks it** — Round 6's gap is closed. Every branch-scope read plant (B1, B3, B3b, B4, C1) and the round-6 tenant-record check (P4) now fail the gates, and the hand attacks found no leak. What remains is a screen-level G2 failure on this piece's own surface. The scope rule says only someone who works in every company may create a company or change a company code (tenancy.companyNeedsEveryCompany). But CompanyForm/CompaniesPage gate New and the Code field on the permission alone (tenancy.companies.create / tenancy.companies.update). They ignore the 'every company' and 'every branch' facts the API already sends for the Workspace screen (everyCompany) and for branches (everyBranch). An administrator limited to some companies or branches is offered both actions, fills a form, and is refused on save. screens.test.tsx models everyCompany only for the Workspace screen, so the gate stays green. This is the company-level twin of round 4's failed branch-level screen gate. It matters for a UAE group because the normal set-up is a group controller who holds every entity and branch or entity managers who hold only their own LLC or FZE. Those managers would meet dead-end New and Code edits on the main master-data screen. Fix: expose an 'every company' flag on the companies list and record (or reuse /tenant's everyCompany). Hide New, the Alt+N key and the command-palette entry, and make Code read-only, unless the user holds every company and every branch of it. Then extend screens.test.tsx with an everyCompany:false case for companies (New absent, field:code absent, Alt+N opens nothing) and plant-test it.

## 2026-10-09T16:17:43Z · p00-foundation · round 8 · WIN

- Commit judged: `bdca699edad3e4d39a21bf22d4829f91c7a9c2f7`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 8
- Biggest gap: **The sign-in win rests on the passkey path alone; signing in with a password ties Odoo on the plain address, and on a shared PC it loses** — Ours beats Odoo only when the person already uses a passkey on this device: 1 confirmation against Odoo's 2 steps. Every first sign-in uses a password, and so does every staff member on a shared counter or warehouse PC. On that path the result is a tie or a loss. (1) Plain address '/': typing a whole e-mail does not move on to the password, so the path is e-mail, Enter, password, Enter: 4 steps and 57 keys for admin@alnoor.example. Odoo's e-mail, Tab, password, Enter is the same 4 steps and 57 keys. Ours wins only on machine seconds. This is round 7's gap, still open. (2) A shared device where anyone once used a passkey (erp.passkeyOffer=1): as the screen opens, it starts a passkey request for up to 300 s, and while that runs the 'Sign in' button is disabled. The page cannot abort the request, so a typed e-mail, Enter, password, Enter does nothing. The next person must first cancel the system prompt: 5 steps against Odoo's 4. For a UAE trading company with shared PCs at the counter and in the warehouse, this is the common case. The fix should let the password path win without a passkey, and must never let the passkey offer block it. Options: abort the arrival request as soon as the person types in the e-mail field (AbortController) and keep Sign in enabled; offer the passkey as a conditional/autofill request (mediation: 'conditional') rather than a modal; move on to the password once the e-mail's domain matches a known workspace on the plain address.
- Gap evidence: attack/shared-device.mjs and shared-device.txt (screenshot 10-shared-device-password-blocked.png): a context with localStorage erp.passkeyOffer=1 and a platform authenticator holding no passkey of this person. On arrival '#passkey-asking' is shown. After typing admin@alnoor.example, Enter, Demo-Pass-2026, Enter, the person is still not signed in after 15 s. web/src/modules/shell/SignInPage.tsx line 417: disabled={busy \|\| asking}. There is no AbortController in SignInPage.tsx or kernel/passkeys.ts. GET /api/auth/session returns passkey.timeout 300000. browser-notes.txt part 2 shows that on plain '/', focus stays in the e-mail field after a whole e-mail is typed. compare/comparisons/sign-in.json gives ours' password paths: new-device 4 steps/35 keys, whole-e-mail 3/56 (team address only), returning 2/21.
