With seven pieces still to add tests, the margin is about 17% on a quiet machine and negative on a busy one. The .NET stage's processor cost is the lever.
  - G1 non-interference cannot see side channels carried by the database. It compares answers from two app processes over the same database, so anything read from PostgreSQL's shared statistics or catalogs (pg_stat_user_tables.n_live_tup, pg_class.reltuples), which erp_app can read for every table, looks identical in both processes. Plant L3 (a list total taken from n_live_tup) was caught only by the HTTP attack's list-walk check. That check fires because the plant changes total without changing the rows. A plant that leaked a whole-database figure somewhere else (an 'activity' counter, a grouping count) would rely on that check alone. Hardening: revoke erp_app's SELECT on the statistics views, or check in the gate that the app role cannot read other tenants' row counts from the catalogs, and add a static ban on pg_stat_* and reltuples in product code.
  - Every critic plant was caught: L1 (raw xmin versions) by G1RowVersionTests, L3 by G1HttpIsolationTests, P1 (read implied by any same-resource permission) by G2 grant-exactly-one, and P2 (no requirement for read permissions) by the host's start-up check. The HTTP isolation test alone took 38 minutes in the planted gate run.
  - Keyboard (round 7's finding, still open): in the shell, Tab after the last navigation link ('My account') lands on the document body before 'Skip to content', a dead stop each cycle (browser-notes.txt part 2). Keyboard sign-in works in English and Arabic. The Arabic shell is RTL with lang=ar. Its only Latin text is company codes, 'Ctrl', the 'English' switch and the workspace code.
  - Sign-in lockout (round 7's finding, still open): after 8 failures, the right password still gets only the generic 'Sign-in failed. Check your e-mail and password and try again.', with no hint that the account is paused or for how long (browser-notes.txt).
  - A passkey added on My account works: a fresh visit to the plain address on that device signs in with no key in 371 ms, recorded as a successful sign-in and audited (insert into identity.passkeys with actor). An empty workspace provisioned in Arabic signs in by keyboard in 0.82 s to an RTL home (09-empty-workspace-first-sign-in.jpg).
  - OpenAPI at /api/openapi/v1.json lists 112 operations (round 7: 101).
  - The schema has no money columns yet (multi-currency is p08). The kernel Money type is decimal with currency, rate, base amount and base currency. docs/compliance/ does not exist; nothing statutory is implemented yet.

## 2026-10-09T16:28:58Z · p04-shell · round 7 · LOSS

- Commit judged: `1e648cadc7d7ae0369979a7c25d8dde195c9ef3e`
- Critic: fresh critic, p04-shell round 7
- Biggest gap: **The command palette cannot run any module action: 'open any screen, record or action by typing' covers only shell actions** — The palette finds screens and records: users, roles, companies and branches (round 4's record gap is fixed). The only actions it lists are the shell's own: switch language, Arabic digits, preferences, shortcuts, navigation pane, print this screen and sign out. Typing 'new', 'new user', 'invite', 'new role', 'new company', 'new branch', 'export', 'copy role', 'unblock' or 'sign-in history' returns nothing. It returns nothing on the Users screen either, where 'new', 'export' and 'columns' also find nothing, though the screen itself has New user (Alt+N), Print or export (Alt+Shift+R), Columns and Views. The cause is the extension contract (web/src/kernel/extensions.ts): modules can give the palette record sources only, with no way to contribute actions. Every later module (invoices, purchase orders, journal entries) will inherit a palette that can find a record but cannot start one or run its commands. For a keyboard-first UAE trading office that is the main promise of the palette. Fix: add a permission-filtered 'actions' contribution to ModuleExtensions (title EN/AR, chord, run or path, optional screen context), register the existing toolbar actions of identity and tenancy (new user, new role, copy role, new company, new branch, print or export the list), and show the current screen's actions first. Add vitest and e2e checks, and a G2 screen plant (an action offered without its permission).

## 2026-10-10T00:06:37Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 9 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `4d34b6aaf982df30fa9c7ef60880e7d947507e99`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 9
- Biggest gap: **Set-up can still do a saved task off the clock: a task that names nothing entered accepts any back-end change as proof, and a save sent from set-up's browser lands inside the measured part** — Round 9 closed the r8 hole only for tasks that name an entered value. Two routes remain.  (1) A task with saves: true and no enters accepts any back-end read that changed during the measured part as the saved state. This covers follow-approval, see-and-rerun-job, import-5000, switch-to-arabic, sign-in and api-update-user. Set-up can do the task, and run() can make one cheap unrelated change, such as a preference or a version. A verify() that gates on that change then answers 'not done' before the clock and 'done' after it. The saved-state rule is satisfied by the unrelated read.  (2) freshStart closes set-up's browser contexts without waiting for requests already sent. A save that set-up's page started lands on the server after the clock starts, and nothing counted caused it. That breaks the README's claim that calls set-up leaves running land before the clock. session.drain covers only fetch, not the browser.  Possible fixes: - For a task that saves, require that the measured part itself sent the change. The tracker should see at least one request that changesProduct (or an op.request that is not GET) during the clock. - Require that the read which changed is the one that holds the task's end state. Tasks could declare what verify() must read (for example a resource path), or every saves task could name an `enters` or an expected value. - Wait for, or refuse, requests still in flight in set-up's browser contexts before closing them. Track them as drain does for fetch. - Also pin X12 (set-up's local storage into a signed-in start) and X16 (clipboard reset) with self-tests. - Add Q1, Q2 and the real-driver plant as self-tests.

## 2026-10-10T05:52:04Z · p05-list-search · round 9 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `35ba5bc1662c614d9d64c70c3e4de7a073b622a8`
- Critic: fresh critic, p05-list-search round 9
- Biggest gap: **The list gates only ever see 25 rows per tenant, so a volume-triggered tenant-blind total (planner estimate from pg_class) passes all of G1** — p05 promises that every list registered with the framework is attacked automatically by G1. Every later module (Contacts, invoices, stock) relies on that promise for its 100,000-row lists. But every gate environment is SeedPlan.Gate with 25 users per tenant. ListAnswers, NonInterference and HttpIsolation judge totals carefully: sorted, keyset, offset, grouped, both directions. They do it only at a volume where no 'large list' code path ever runs. The most natural optimisation for a 100,000-row list is to stop counting exactly and show an estimate. The estimate PostgreSQL offers (pg_class.reltuples, pg_stat_user_tables.n_live_tup, EXPLAIN row estimates) covers every tenant, because row-level security does not apply to catalog statistics, and erp_app can read them. Plant L12 does exactly this past 10,000 rows. Nothing structural flags it either: ProcessState only sees in-process state, and TenantSource and ReviewedLookup do not look at catalog reads. Fixes: (a) judge list answers also against a tenant pair where both tenants hold a demo volume of rows (the timing stage's 100,000-row fixture exists already). Sending each list's unfiltered, sorted, offset and grouped query as the smaller tenant, the total must equal its own row count. (b) Add a source or SQL-trace rule: product code and the SQL it runs in a request may not read pg_class, pg_stat*, pg_statistic or EXPLAIN output, unless that is reviewed. (c) Keep the volume threshold out of reach of a gate that only seeds tens of rows: give the gate a 'large list' profile.
- Gap evidence: Apply gauntlet/evidence/p05-list-search/r9/L12-estimated-total-from-table-statistics.patch to 35ba5bc. ## 2026-10-10T06:52:00Z · p03-identity · round 8 · LOSS

- Commit judged: `35ba5bc1662c614d9d64c70c3e4de7a073b622a8`
- Critic: fresh critic, p03-identity round 8
- Biggest gap: **A lost or stolen device keeps signing in: 'Reset password' and 'Sign out everywhere' leave the user's passkeys working, and no administrator screen shows or removes them** — This round added passkeys: a user adds one in My account and then signs in with one device confirmation. The administrator's levers on the users screen did not grow with it.  When an employee's laptop is lost or stolen, an administrator does what the user panel offers: 'Reset password…' ("Password reset. N sessions ended.") and 'Sign out everywhere'. The passkey on the lost laptop still signs in straight afterwards (200).  The user panel's tabs (Details, What they can do, Sign-in history) never mention passkeys. The sign-in history does not say whether a sign-in used a passkey or a password. The API has GET and DELETE /api/identity/users/{id}/passkeys, summarised as being 'for a lost device, a person leaving', but no screen calls them. The only screen lever that works is deactivating the account, which also locks out the person.  For a UAE trading company with shared or field laptops, this makes the scope's 'reset password, lockout' unsafe for exactly the case it exists for.  Fix: - Show a user's passkeys (name, created, last used) in the user panel, with 'Remove all passkeys' for users within the caller's access. That is the existing DELETE, Arabic included. - Have 'Reset password' (or a tick box on it) and 'Sign out everywhere' offer to remove passkeys too, and say so in the done message. - Record the sign-in method in the sign-in history. - Add an e2e test: after reset plus sign-out-everywhere plus remove, the device's passkey answers 401.
- Gap evidence: In the seeded demo (./erp up), run node gauntlet/evidence/p03-identity/r8/attacks/lost-device.mjs (uses softpasskey.mjs) against the demo URL. Output (attacks/lost-device.txt): - 'user adds passkey: 201' - 'admin resets password: 200' - 'admin ends sessions: 200' - 'lost device signs in with its passkey after reset + end sessions: 200 r8.lost.…@alnoor.example' - 'API: admin lists that user's passkeys: [{"name":"Laptop",…}]' - Only deactivation stops it: 'passkey sign-in after deactivation: 401'  Screen: walks/walk7.mjs opens the viewer, who holds the passkey 'Critic virtual key' added in walk6 through a Chromium virtual authenticator. It reports "tabs mentioning a passkey: []" (screenshot 12). web/src/modules/identity/UserPanel.tsx has 0 occurrences of 'passkey', and the only callers of /passkeys are in PasskeysSection.tsx (the user's own).
- Hard gates:
  - G1 tenant isolation of the product as built: **PASS** — No leak found. As alnoor's admin I attacked gulfsteel with 96 calls (attacks/g1-attack.mjs, g1-a-vs-b.txt). The targets were B's admin and 3 users (GET record, access, sign-ins, default-company, passkeys; PUT; password; unblock; sessions/revoke; default-company PUT; DELETE passkeys; DELETE), 3 of B's roles (GET, PUT, copy, DELETE), B's new passkey through /me/passkeys/{id} (GET, PUT, DELETE), and B's saved and shared list views (GET, PUT, DELETE). Every call answered 404 or 400. Tenant-switch headers and query fields (x-tenant-id, x-tenant, x-workspace, ?tenant=, ?workspace=) were ignored. A hash of B's users, roles, user_roles and passkeys was the same before and after.  Passkey sign-in: I used a software ES256 authenticator I wrote (attacks/softpasskey.mjs). A's key under handles naming B's admin, A's key with B's credential id, and A's key with a handle naming no workspace all gave byte-identical 401 auth.passkeyFailed answers. B's own passkey signs in (200), and replaying the same answer is refused (401).  Differential checks: B's ids against random ids in view PUT, roleIds, companyRoles[].companyId, the role filter and the usersByRole report parameter. Every pair answered the same (g1-differential.txt).  Exports: users, roles, usersByRole and roleSummary as CSV, XLSX (unzipped) and PDF, in English and Arabic, held no gulfsteel data.  Database (db-app-role.txt), as erp_app: - With no tenant bound, and with a session-wide set_config, it sees 0 rows. - Bound to A, it sees 100,005 users and 1 passkey, 0 of them from other tenants. - UPDATE of B's users and passkeys changed 0 rows. An INSERT into B's passkeys was refused by RLS. - DISABLE RLS, SET ROLE erp_owner and row_security=off were all refused. No erp role has bypassrls. - identity.passkeys has RLS enabled and forced, with only the standard tenant_isolation policy.
  - G1 gate catches tenant leaks planted in this piece's surface: **PASS** — Plants are in plants/apply-r8-plants.py and plants/r8-plants-run1.diff. They ran through verify-slot (plants/run1/gates.log). - L7, a database leak: an extra PERMISSIVE SELECT policy on identity.passkeys that opens every workspace's passkeys when no tenant is bound ('so passkey sign-in finds the credential first'). Caught by G1DatabaseIsolationTests ('identity.passkeys: 2 rows visible without a tenant' and 'unreviewed policy passkeys_before_workspace'), G1TenantSettingCodeTests and G1TenantSourceTests. - L8, a process-state leak: the PasskeyChallenges singleton remembers offered credentials per e-mail address and adds them to excludeCredentials, so a same-address user in tenant A is handed tenant B's credential ids. Caught by G1ProcessStateTests ('singleton ...PasskeyChallenges._offered: a read-only field that holds a mutable ConcurrentDictionary') and by G1HttpIsolationTests ('Process-wide state changed while the tenants used the app ... _offered[admin@bravo-...]').
  - G2 permissions of the product as built (restricted roles, escalation, screens hide): **PASS** — Browser walk (walks/walk2-restricted-user.txt): - A user created by keyboard with the Read-only role signs in with the one-time code and sets a password. - That user sees no New user button. - Commit judged: `affe604dc0bbc7d7a6c7cb77f6828b5abe1c06f8`
- Critic: fresh critic, p03-identity round 9
- Biggest gap: **The takeover gate never sends documented query parameters, so the new lost-device lever 'Sign out everywhere ?removePasskeys=true' can skip its target check and every gate stays green** — Round 8's gap is fixed in the product. An administrator now sees a user's passkeys in the user panel and can remove one, or all. 'Sign out everywhere' asks first, with 'Also remove their N passkeys' ticked, and 'Reset password' has a tick box to remove them too. The sign-in history shows Password or Passkey. A lost laptop's passkey answers 401 afterwards, in English and Arabic, by keyboard.  The new levers are switched by a query parameter (?removePasskeys=true, ?passkeyId=), and the G2 takeover gate never sends one. G2AccountTakeoverTests builds every request from the path and the JSON body only (endpoint.Path(...) plus BodyAsync).  Plant P4 makes RevokeSessions skip TargetProblemAsync when removePasskeys is true. With it, a helpdesk user holding only identity.users.read/update can do this to the Administrator: - end every one of their sessions; - strip every one of their passkeys.  The whole .NET suite stays green, and so do web and e2e, which do not touch this server path. The product is correct today: the unplanted proof passes, and my live probe answers 403. Nothing stops a regression, though, and that one line is exactly the kind of 'emergency shortcut' a lost-device feature invites.  Fix: - Make the takeover, company-caller and grant-bearing-record checks send every documented query parameter in each variant: booleans true and false, ids from the target and from a stronger user (passkeyId of the Administrator's key under a weak target). - Add P4, and P5 as a gate rather than only a functional test, as LeakyModule self-tests. - Do the same for request-body flags such as ResetPasswordRequest.removePasskeys.

## 2026-10-10T13:11:34Z · p06-form-report · round 5 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `76ab5f8f40debea1e7a2d82a1828ac480da684fe`
- Critic: fresh critic, p06-form-report round 5
- Biggest gap: **The gates still try prints one parameter at a time: a record PDF kept for the inline viewer leaks one tenant's company profile to another, a saved-view print names roles to a clerk who may not read them, and every gate passes** — For a UAE trading company, prints and exports are how data leaves the system, and real prints combine parameters. The screen's PDF viewer sends disposition=inline with a record id, and a saved view sends filter, sort and columns together. Round 5 closed round 4's single-parameter gaps: G1's victim and G2ReportData now print with each documented value. But each is still printed alone. G1 never sends tenant B's record id together with any non-default print parameter (disposition, numerals, timeZone, groupBy). The probe sends only format and language, and the answer shapes use each tenant's own ids. G2 never combines two parameters. So any store or shortcut that keys on a combination is invisible. The fix: (1) in G1, tenant B prints each record document and parameterised report with its own record ids across the answer-shape cover (every enumerated value, plus documented values combined, not one per print). Tenant A then asks for those exact requests, tenant B's ids included, with and without headers. (2) In G2ReportData, print with a covering set of documented-value combinations (at least every pair, plus the screen's own saved-view shape: search+filter+sort+columns+groupBy) for every permission set. (3) Plant-test both with L7b and P10. Either gate fix also applies beyond reports, wherever an answer depends on two inputs together.

## 2026-10-10T13:17:01Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 10 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `c43966c`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 10
- Biggest gap: **The instrument credits the person with whatever the page sends during the clock: set-up can stash the task's value or trigger in the product, and the start page's own code makes the declared write** — Round 10 requires that a task which saves sends one of its declared writes during the measured part. It also requires that every entered value its end state gained was 'typed, picked as a file, or sent in one of its writes'. Both rules look at what the browser page sent, not at what a counted step caused.  So set-up, which may write to the product before the clock, can leave the work for the product's own page code: - a server-side draft holding the value, which the start screen offers to save with one click (R1: 1 step, 0 keys), or - a setting that makes the start page send the declared write from a timer (R2, and R3 for an entered value: 1 step, 1 key).  The start-screen check looks only at field values, so a value shown as text ('Draft restored: abcdefghij') passes. This is the same off-the-clock class as r9's Q1, Q2 and round 10's Q3/Q3c, moved from the back end into the page.  Possible fixes: (a) For a browser task, an entered value counts as entered only when the measured part typed it, picked it as a file, or chose it on screen. Its mere presence in a request body the page composed does not count. (b) The declared write must be caused by a counted step. Read Chromium's request initiator (CDP Network.requestWillBeSent initiator stack or user activation), or require the write to start while a step is being performed or settled, never from a timer. (c) The start-screen check reads the screen's text, not only its fields, for entered values. (d) Refuse a set-up write whose body carries an entered value. Add R1-R3 as self-tests and mutations.

<!-- verdict:p02-tenancy--r009--ae5e3fb7bb -->
## 2026-10-11T06:00:44Z · p02-tenancy · round 9 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `ae5e3fb7bbf8deb67e84603ae114f8fb1ad0272a`
- Critic: fresh critic, p02-tenancy round 9
- Biggest gap: **The Companies screen gate never models a user who works in no company: rewriting its 'every company' read as `page.items[0]?.everyCompany !== false` offers New to every newly invited administrator, the server refuses it, and every web test stays green** — Round 7's gap (New and Code offered to an administrator of some companies) and round 8's gap (PU, a default-company change for a user who also works elsewhere) are both closed. On the real build, New, Alt+N, ?open=new and the palette are absent for a one-company and a one-branch administrator, and Code is read-only with its reason (walk-limited.log, shots 04 and 05). PU is caught by the new contract test, and PU2 by both the contract test and G2AccountTakeover. The remaining fault is in CompaniesPage.useEveryCompany. It decides 'works in every company' from the first row of GET /api/tenancy/companies?take=1, and it is right only because of `page.items.length > 0 &&`. screens.test.tsx always serves one company row, so it never models a user whose list is empty. The natural rewrite `page.items[0]?.everyCompany !== false` turns an empty list into 'every company'. That state is not exotic: a user created with the Administrator role has no company access until someone grants it, and an administrator of one company can remove someone's last company. Such a user sees New on Companies, fills the form, and is refused on save. That is the same dead-end class as round 7, for the first screen a newly invited finance or admin lead of a UAE group opens. Fix: in screens.test.tsx, add a companies case where the list answers no rows (with every permission): New absent, Alt+N and ?open=new open nothing. ## 2026-10-11T06:44:15Z · p00-foundation · round 9 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `99e596bf256d28bcdd9c5d3f99031aa17e8abe72`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 9
- Biggest gap: **The permission gate (G2) checks only cookie sessions, so a kernel fault that lets any Bearer-token caller skip every permission check passes all 1,501 tests** — Signing in with issueToken:true gives any user a Bearer token, and the API accepts it on every endpoint. G2's deny-by-default test ('a user whose roles grant nothing is denied everything') and its 'granting exactly one permission opens exactly the endpoints that declare it' test sign in only through Env.SignInAsync, which is cookie-based. Bearer sessions appear only for administrators, in G1 and in one identity test, and an administrator holds every permission anyway. A plant in the kernel's PermissionAuthorizationHandler lets any Bearer caller pass every requirement. With it, a no-roles user reads all 100,000 users and reaches every write's own business checks. G1, G2, the rule gates and every module and kernel test still pass. For a UAE company that connects an integration (e-commerce, a POS or a bank feed) through the API, this is the most likely path to over-privilege, and nothing would detect it. Fix: run both G2 matrices (no roles denied everything; exactly one permission opens exactly its endpoints) once per credential carrier: cookie, Bearer, and any carrier added later. Make the carrier list come from SessionAuthenticationHandler.ReadToken, or a reviewed list, so a new carrier cannot be left out. Add a self-test that plants this bypass and must fail.
- Gap evidence: plants/plants.diff, P3 hunk in src/Kernel/Erp.Kernel/Security/Permissions.cs, active only when CRITIC_PLANT=P3. Run plants/run-plants.sh in the toolbox (erp-toolbox:pw1.63.0-net10, mounted as in ./erp run_toolbox) with PLANTS='P3 P3G1 P3M P3PROOF P3NONE'. Results: plant-P3.log, G2 36 of 36 passed; plant-P3G1.log, G1 and Rules 99 tests, whose only failure is the inert text of plant L4; plant-P3M-*.log, 409 module and kernel tests all passed; plant-P3PROOF.log, CriticP3Proof.cs failed with 'no-roles user with a bearer token: GET /api/identity/users -> 200, POST /api/identity/roles -> 403 identity.grantBeyondOwn'; plant-P3NONE.log, the same test passes without the plant. Source: tests/Erp.Gates.Tests/G2/G2PermissionTests.cs lines 324-389 use Env.SignInAsync only, and grep finds no SignInWithTokenAsync in tests/Erp.Gates.Tests/G2.

  - Processor time: verify used 10,313 s against the owner's 10,500 s maximum, a 1.8% margin, with load up to 95 on 16 CPUs; dotnet alone used 8,214 s. Round 8's two runs used 11,671 s (busy) and 8,694 s (quiet), so this round's processor time cut kept a busy run under the maximum, but only just. Wall time was 5,016 s and was not judged (busy machine).
  - Kernel Money disagrees with the signed-off rule docs/compliance/vat-rounding-and-currency.md, rule 2 ('Lines and tax-category subtotals keep their full precision; the invoice total is rounded'). (a) Money.Create refuses any amount with more decimals than the currency's minor units, so a line cannot keep full precision. (b) Money.Create rounds every base amount to the fils, and Money.Add sums those rounded base amounts. Example: three lines of USD 1.00 at 3.6725 add up to a base of 11.01 AED, while converting the 3.00 USD total once gives 11.02. The MinorUnits table is also written from memory, not from docs/compliance (src/Kernel/Erp.Kernel/Money/Money.cs lines 24-29 and 90-96, 104-109). Nothing uses Money yet, so this can be fixed before p08 builds on it.

After a password sign-in the device stops asking on arrival (value '2'). The remembered-domain feature stores only salted hashes of domains, and it moves on only for domains this device signed in to: another workspace's address does not move on.

In the Arabic shell, the only Latin text is company codes, 'Ctrl', the 'English' switch and the workspace code. An empty workspace provisioned in Arabic signs in by keyboard in 0.87 s to an RTL home. The no-roles user sees 'No areas are open to you yet' and gets 403 from the API.

## 2026-10-11T09:27:31Z · p04-shell · round 8 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `8bc6a09`
- Critic: fresh critic, p04-shell round 8
- Biggest gap: **A cross-tenant leak with no marker on the shell's own preferences endpoint passes every G1 gate, because the non-interference check never compares response headers** — G1NonInterference is the check meant to catch leaks that carry nothing recognisable of tenant B's: a count, an order, a timing. It compares only the status code and the body of each answer (NonInterference.GetAsync returns '<status> <normalised body>', and the write comparisons are built the same way). The marker-based HTTP attack judges headers, but only for B's ids, e-mails, codes and canaries. G1ProcessState inspects static fields and walks product singletons, but not values written into framework singletons such as IConfiguration. So a process-wide count, sent back in a response header and stored outside a static field, is invisible to all three. I planted exactly that on PUT /api/identity/me/preferences, the shell's own endpoint and its Arabic path: it returns how many Arabic switches the whole process has seen, across tenants. All 60 G1 tests passed, and on the running build tenant A can watch tenant B's activity. This is the kind of leak real code produces, such as rate-limit headers, an ETag or version counter, or X-Total-Count kept in a shared cache. Every later module (invoices, stock, journals) will add headers and caches, and they inherit the same blind spot. The fix lies in the shared G1 machinery (owned with p00), but it fails this round because the fault is on p04's surface. Fix: (1) In NonInterference, compare normalised response headers for GETs and writes, dropping Date, trace and request ids and normalising Set-Cookie values, and add a ratchet count of compared headers. (2) In G1ProcessState, snapshot the in-memory values of IConfiguration and the other framework singletons before and after both tenants' activity, or make runtime writes to IConfiguration fail. (3) Add this plant to the self-noninterference self-tests so it stays caught.
- Gap evidence: In a clone at 8bc6a09, apply gauntlet/evidence/p04-shell/r8/plant-server-L3-count-header.diff (ProfileEndpoints.UpdatePreferences: on language=ar or numerals=arab, increment IConfiguration['Erp:ArabicSwitches'] and return it in the header X-Arabic-Switches). Run dotnet test tests/Erp.Gates.Tests with filter 'FullyQualifiedName~Erp.Gates.Tests.G1&Load!=Timing&Process!=self-http&Process!=self-company&Process!=self-noninterference'. ## 2026-10-16T23:29:56Z · p00-foundation · round 10 · WIN

- Commit judged: `c4cfacb59392c0b536df17215b0498d9554cf1cc`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 10
- Biggest gap: **Kernel Money adds base amounts that were already rounded, against the owner-signed rounding rule, so foreign-currency documents can be off by fils** — Every module that posts foreign-currency amounts (purchases in USD or EUR, the everyday case for a UAE trading company) will build on the kernel Money type. docs/compliance/vat-rounding-and-currency.md rule 2, signed off by the owner, says: 'Rounding happens once, on the figure that is stated. Lines and tax-category subtotals keep their full precision; the invoice total is rounded to 2 decimals.' The kernel breaks this in two ways. (a) Money.Create rounds each base amount to the fils, and Money.Add sums those rounded amounts (BaseAmount + other.BaseAmount). Example: three lines of USD 1.00 at 3.6725 give 3.67 + 3.67 + 3.67 = AED 11.01, but the rule gives 11.0175, rounded to 11.02. (b) Money.Create throws on any amount with more decimals than the currency's minor units, so a line cannot keep full precision at all. Round 9 reported this and it is still open (round 10 only factored out RoundToMinorUnits). Fix in the kernel before p08 and the ledger build on it: keep line and subtotal base amounts at full precision (or carry the unrounded product), round only the stated total, and have the type report the rounding difference that e-invoices put in 'Rounding Amount'. This is a rule already signed off, so implementing it needs no new human gate.
- Gap evidence: git show c4cfacb:src/Kernel/Erp.Kernel/Money/Money.cs: line 93 throws when an amount has more decimals than the currency's minor units, line 96 rounds the base amount on creation, line 117 'BaseAmount + other.BaseAmount'. Compare with git show c4cfacb:docs/compliance/vat-rounding-and-currency.md, Rule 2. To reproduce in any test: var l = Money.Create(1.00m, USD, 3.6725m, AED); l.Add(l).Add(l).BaseAmount == 11.01m, while rule 2 gives 11.02m.

## 2026-10-17T00:28:28Z · p04-shell · round 9 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `a2c3400178abeb27ffe24b7005ec8d68dde172ba`
- Critic: fresh critic, p04-shell round 9
- Biggest gap: **The G1 non-interference check still misses a cross-tenant leak that carries no marker when it travels in a cookie value or as a time in a response header: it blanks every Set-Cookie value and every time before comparing** — Round 9 closed round 8's gap: response headers are now compared, configuration is sealed, and a changing count in a header is caught by the change probe (my L5 was caught). But NonInterference.NormalizeHeaders still replaces the value of every Set-Cookie with <value> and every time in any header with <time>. The change probe compares the same normalised answers, so it sees no change either. A process-wide count sent in a cookie, or the time of another tenant's last action sent in a header, is invisible to all three G1 checks. The marker attack looks only for B's ids and names, and ProcessState does not see state kept outside the process (a temp file, a shared cache). I planted both kinds on PUT /api/identity/me/preferences, the shell's own Arabic-switch endpoint. All 169 gate tests passed, and on the running build tenant A watched tenant B's activity: A's count went from 2 to 8, and A saw the exact time of B's last switch. Real code produces exactly this: rate-limit or 'last activity' cookies, Last-Modified or ETag-style timestamps of shared caches, and 'recently changed' stamps. Every later module (invoices, stock, journals) inherits the blind spot. The machinery is shared with p00, but the fault sits on p04's surface, as in r8. Fix: (1) In NormalizeHeaders, blank only the values of the product's own reviewed credential cookies (the session and trusted-device cookies, from a reviewed list). Compare every other cookie value, and feed changing values to the change probe. (2) Replace times only in Date and the declared print stamp. Leave every other time in a header as sent, and judge it by how it changes (alone against with the other tenant in between, with a pause between calls). Apply the same to times in bodies. (3) Add both plants to the non-interference self-tests, with a ratchet count of cookie values compared.
- Gap evidence: In a clone at a2c3400, apply gauntlet/evidence/p04-shell/r9/plants/plant-server-B1-L4-L6.diff. The normaliser is in tests/Erp.Gates.Tests/G1/NonInterference.cs: NormalizeHeaders, CookieValueRegex, and TimeRegex/HttpDateRegex applied to every header.

No gate checks that a request authorised by permission P writes only the records P covers. I planted P1b in WorkplaceEndpoints.Switch: SwitchWorkplaceRequest gains `bool? ArabicLegalNameRequired`, and the handler sets it on the target company when the caller works in every branch of it. With that plant, any user who may switch company can switch on the company's Arabic-legal-name requirement, a legal setting that otherwise needs tenancy.companies.update. A UAE group gives every clerk the switch permission, so any such side door is open to the whole staff. Fix: give each permission a reviewed write set, for example tenancy.workplace.switch -> tenancy.user_workplaces only, and tenancy.companies.update -> tenancy.companies minus the logo columns. During G2's 'granting exactly one permission' run, read audit.entries by correlation id and fail any request under P that writes a table or column outside P's set. Add P1b to the gate self-tests.

- Biggest gap: **Edit and save only ties Odoo's shortest path: a triple-click in Odoo gives 3 steps and 17 keys, and ours' best is 3 steps and 18 keys (or 17 by pointer)** — Editing a record is the most frequent thing every later module will do with this form framework: a phone number, a price, a quantity. Against Odoo's real expert path the form no longer wins. The harness drives Odoo with click > Ctrl+A > type > Save (4 steps, 19 keys), so it scores ours (click > type > Enter: 3 steps, 18 keys) as a win. An Odoo expert instead triple-clicks the field, types and clicks Save: 3 steps, 17 keys. I verified this path live on the reference rig. Ours ties on steps on every path. On keystrokes it loses (18, Enter path) or ties (17, pointer path). A tie is a loss. Ours needs a path that is strictly shorter in both. The UAE-specific way is a phone field type in the form kernel that keeps the country code (+971) and takes only the national number. The task would then need 10 digits plus spaces instead of 16 characters and Shift, which is clear of any Odoo path, and every later module's phone fields would gain the same. (A general alternative: save with no key and no step, for example save-on-leave, but that changes the dirty/leave model.) ## 2026-10-17T09:40:26Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 11 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `7308c90650c33f3e70d268605607c330999abaae`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 11
- Biggest gap: **A step's 'cause' window still covers time the step only spent waiting, so the page's own timer write is credited to one click. The product can also tell when the clock starts from verify()'s before-the-clock read.** — Round 11 judges causation by timing alone. A write counts as caused when it goes out between a step's start and 1 s after its end, or within 1 s of the answer to any request that went out during a step. Three things defeat this.  (1) A step's window is [at, at+took], and 'took' includes Playwright's actionability wait. An op.click (or op.scrollTo) on an element that only appears after the page's own timer has sent the declared write covers that write. This is round 10's R2 again, with one click and 0 keys (S1, S1b).  (2) The one-link rule accepts any slow answer as the cause. A delay the step arms is refused as a timer (R2b), but the same delay put into a slow back-end answer is accepted (S2).  (3) verify() always reads the back end just before op.start(), so the product sees the clock about to start. It can let the page's ordinary poll approve 0.6 s later; one click on plain text then 'causes' it, and the whole run takes 0.47 s (S3).  All four are recorded as verified and beat Odoo's follow-approval baseline.  Possible fixes: (a) Time a pointer step from the moment its input is dispatched, not from when the operator began waiting. For example, wait for actionability first (a trial click, or a waitFor counted as system wait), then record `at` just before the real click. (b) Require causal evidence. Use Chromium's request initiator (CDP Network.requestWillBeSent initiator stack, or a script stack rooted in a trusted input event handler), not proximity in time. Link through an answer only when the request carrying it was itself caused by the step. (c) Put an unpredictable gap, or a fixed wait longer than REACTION_SECONDS, between the before-clock verify() read and op.start(). Or read verify's before state without a back-end call the product can tell apart. (d) Add S1-S3 as self-tests and mutations. (e) Add a self-test that a step's label never counts as typed text (my mutation C3 survives the whole suite).
- Gap evidence: Copy gauntlet/evidence/p01-odoo-rig/r11/plants/zz-critic-r11.test.mjs to gauntlet/compare/test/, then run: cd gauntlet/compare && npm ci && node data/generate.mjs && node --test --test-concurrency=1 test/zz-critic-r11.test.mjs Control C1 passes. S1, S1b, S2 and S3 fail with 'MISSED ... verified with 1 steps, 0 keys ... the comparison says win' (plants/critic-r11-plants.log). The RESULT lines: - S1: steps=1 keys=0 machine=2.882 writes_caused=[{POST /api/approve, by_step 1}] - S1b: steps=1 keys=0 machine=2.83 - S2: steps=1 keys=0 by_step 1 after_answer_to GET /api/tick - S3: steps=1 keys=0 machine=0.466 Mutation C3: node scripts/critic-r11-mutations.mjs C3 --all-files (plants/critic-r11-mutations.mjs) prints 'C3 a step's label counts as typed text: MISSED [files: all]' (plants/critic-r11-mutations-fullsuite.log).
- Hard gates:
  - Instrument integrity: the harness catches faults planted in the measuring instrument (this piece's surface): **FAIL** — plants/zz-critic-r11.test.mjs, log in plants/critic-r11-plants.log. The control C1 (an honest approval click) verifies. Four plants are MISSED. Each is recorded as 'verified', and each beats Odoo's real follow-approval baseline (4 steps, 0 keys, 3.521 s, 10.6 s): compareRuns says 'win'. - S1: set-up turns on a setting, and the start page sends the declared POST /api/approve on its own 3 s timer (round 10's R2). Then a 'Close' banner appears. run() = op.click('#close') only. Playwright's click waits for its target to appear, and that wait sits inside the step's window [at, at+took]. So the timer's write went out 'while a counted step was being performed'. Result: verified, 1 step, 0 keys, 2.882 s, writes_caused [{POST /api/approve, by_step 1}]. - S1b: the same with one op.scrollTo('#close'). Result: verified, 1 step, 0 keys. - S2: R2b (a delay the first input arms) with the 2.5 s timer moved into a slow back-end answer. The first pointer press makes the page send GET /api/tick; the stand-in answers after 2.5 s, and the page approves. The 'one link' rule credits the click (after_answer_to GET /api/tick). Result: 1 step, 0 keys. - S3: no step drives the page's timing at all. The stand-in arms itself when verify()'s before-the-clock read arrives, which is the runner's last act before the clock. - Critic: fresh critic, p03-identity round 10
- Biggest gap: **The permission matrix shrinks every 'Other' permission's checkbox to a dot, so the most sensitive grants are the hardest to see and click when building a restricted role** — In Roles, the matrix's 'Other' column holds the permissions that are not view, create, change or delete. Today these are 'Reset other users' passwords' and 'Share list views with everyone'. As modules arrive, this column will hold post journal, approve, close period and the like.  There, the checkbox renders 4.5 to 11.8 px wide, against 13 px for every other checkbox. It looks like a list bullet ('◦'), not a box, at every common screen width. In the system Administrator role, the ticked state is almost invisible.  An administrator setting up a 'Sales clerk' or 'Storekeeper' role in a UAE trading or manufacturing company cannot see at a glance whether the risky grants are on. With a mouse they must hit a target a few pixels wide. Odoo's group and access screens show such rights as ordinary full-size toggles.  Keyboard toggling works (Space), and the API is correct. So this is the screen failing the 'dense, Dynamics-style business screen' promise exactly where the stakes are highest.  Cause: web/src/modules/identity/identity.css, .id-cell is display:inline-flex. The checkbox keeps the default flex-shrink:1 and is squeezed when the label beside it wraps.  Fix: give the checkbox flex: 0 0 auto (or put the label under it). Add a screen test that every matrix checkbox renders at full size at 1280, 1600 and 1920 px.

## 2026-10-17T14:41:34Z · p06-form-report · round 7 · LOSS

- Commit judged: `08159a4f`
- Critic: fresh critic, p06-form-report round 7
- Biggest gap: **Edit and save still ties Odoo on keystrokes: Odoo's shortest path takes the same compact number, so it is 3 steps and 14 keys against ours' 2 steps and 14 keys** — The new phone field and the completed-entry save remove the save key and a step, so ours now wins steps, machine seconds and modelled seconds. Keystrokes still tie. Ours' credited path types +971505550199 (14 keys); the instrument requires +971 to be typed. Odoo's real expert path types the same 14 keys: triple-click the phone field, type +971505550199, click Save. Odoo shows '+971 50 555 0199' on the form, stores '+971505550199', and the instrument credits that entry ('separators free').  The harness misses this for two reasons. The Odoo driver types the spaced number and its verify() demands the exact spaced string, which is stricter than the instrument's own rule. And the instrument still cannot express a triple-click. A tie is a loss, and editing a field is the most frequent action every later module will build on this form.  The digits themselves cannot be cut, so ours needs a credited path that is strictly shorter in keys. The clearest one: ours already supplies +971, so the person types only the 9-digit national number (505550199, 9 keys), and ours stores the full international number. Odoo's 9-digit entry stores just '505550199', without the country code, so Odoo cannot be credited for it, and Odoo stays at 14. For that path to count, the measuring instrument must accept a fixed prefix that a field shows as its own, before the clock, as not needing to be typed. That decision belongs to p01 and the lead; ours' driver comment already describes the 9-digit path. A product alternative is any entry ours can do in fewer keys than 14 that the instrument credits.
- Gap evidence: Hand check of Odoo: tools/odoo-compact-phone.mjs (run against localhost:8069, db reference, admin; needle C073519) and its output compare-hand/odoo-compact-phone.txt. Triple-click selection [0,16,16]. Typing '+971505550199' gives shown '+971 50 555 0199' and stored '+971505550199'. Typing '505550199' or '0505550199' also shows '+971 50 555 0199'; tools/odoo-raw-phone-display.mjs confirms the list view shows the same. The contact was restored to '+971 54 427 3034' after each run.  Instrument acceptance: tools/odoo-edit-and-save-compact-driver.mjs (clone-only Odoo driver variant) run with `node run.mjs --task edit-and-save --product odoo --repeat 3`. Results in compare-odoo-compact/results/*.json: verified, 4 steps/16 keys, steps click > Control+a (2 keys) > type '+971505550199' (14 keys) > click Save, saved_state.end_state_gained ['+971 50 555 0199'].  Ours: compare/comparisons/edit-and-save.json, ours_path 'compact', 2 steps, 14 keys, 0.227 s, 6.97 s modelled. Screenshot 10 shows Odoo's form with the compact entry displayed grouped (branding cropped).

## 2026-10-17T16:45:31Z · p03-identity · round 11 · WIN

- Commit judged: `65a646dfbd732540cd19a9b65914f9750cf006b0`
- Critic: fresh critic, p03-identity round 11
- Biggest gap: **In the shipped deployment every client has the same address, so five anonymous wrong passwords stop the real user signing in from any new device for 15 minutes, and the sign-in history cannot tell attacker from owner** — The README promises: 'Failed sign-ins pause only the client that failed, on that account.' In the one-command deployment the app sees every request from the compose gateway (172.21.0.1 or 172.22.0.1). Only a browser that has signed in to that account before is told apart, by its device cookie.  An anonymous attacker who knows an e-mail address sends 5 wrong passwords with no session. After that, the owner's correct password from a new laptop, a fresh browser, or cleared cookies answers 429 with Retry-After 900. The message even says 'from this device'.  The set-up-code path is a fresh browser by definition. So a newly invited employee, or the finance manager on a replacement laptop, can be kept out indefinitely at 5 requests every 15 minutes.  The administrator's tools cannot help: - The sign-in history and 'Sign-in is paused from 1 address' show the same 172.x address for the attacker's script and the owner's laptop. The user agent is the only difference. - 'Allow sign-in again' lifts the pause for the attacker too.  This has been an 'other finding' since round 1. It is now the largest p03 weakness left, because lockout and sign-in history are in this piece's scope and everything else in scope works.  Fix: - Make the shipped deployment see real client addresses: put a reverse proxy in compose with ERP_KNOWN_PROXIES set by default, or use host networking for the published port. Show in the history when an address is a proxy or gateway. - Until then, do not let failures from an unrecognised client pause a first sign-in that presents a valid set-up code. - Add a test: a second client with no device cookie, behind the same gateway, still signs in after another client's 5 failures.
- Gap evidence: 1. ./erp up (any ports). 2. node attacks/pause-dos.mjs. It sends 5 wrong passwords for viewer@alnoor.example with user agent 'attacker-script/1.0', then the right password with a different user agent and no cookie.  Output (attacks/pause-dos.txt): - 'attacker attempt 1..4: 401', 'attempt 5: 429'; - 'the owner from a new laptop (no device cookie), right password: 429 retry-after 900 ... Too many failed sign-ins with this e-mail address from this device'; - 'history addresses: throttled@172.21.0.1 Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0)', then 'failed@172.21.0.1 attacker-script/1.0' x5; - 'paused clients: [{source: 172.21.0.1, failures: 5}]'.  The same address in the UI: screenshot 09-sign-in-history-paused-client-en.jpg ('Sign-in is paused from 1 address ... 172.22.0.1'). X-Forwarded-For is rightly ignored (walks/w5-unblock-palette.txt).
- Hard gates:

They used each of B's ids (users, roles, companies, branches, views, tenant, canary user and role) in the path and in body fields. They also tried ?removePasskeys=true, ?passkeyId=, the tenant-switch headers (X-Tenant, X-Tenant-Id, X-Erp-Tenant, X-Workspace), tenant query fields, every list export (CSV, XLSX, PDF; English and Arabic) and the four reports with B's role and company ids. - XLSX files were unzipped before searching. - PDFs were text-extracted with pypdf (pdf-attack.txt): 36 PDFs, 0 leaks. Two controls confirmed the extraction works: B's own export does contain its canary, and A's export contains alnoor. - A hash of B's identity state was the same before and after.  Existence oracle: for all 80 id routes and bodies, a B id answers exactly as a random id does (oracle-all.txt, view-oracle.txt).  Database, as erp_app (db-app-role.txt): - With no tenant bound, 0 rows. - Bound to A: 100,005 users, 200,074 audit rows, 0 from B. - UPDATE and DELETE on B's rows changed 0 rows. An INSERT for B was refused by RLS. - row_security=off, SET ROLE erp_owner/postgres/erp_auth and DISABLE RLS were all refused. - All 17 tenant tables have RLS enabled and forced, and no erp role has bypassrls. - The only security-definer functions erp_app can run are verify_sign_in and resolve_session. resolve_session needs a token hash and refuses inside a tenant-bound transaction.  No files or background jobs exist yet beyond company logos, which the API attack covered.

Escalation (attacks/escalate.txt): a helpdesk role holds users read/update/create and roles read/update/create, and works in ALN-DXB. Every escalation attempt was refused with 403: - giving themselves Administrator, everywhere or in one company; - adding resetPassword or tenant.update to their own role; - creating a role beyond their own permissions; - copying Administrator; - giving Administrator to another user, everywhere or in their shared company, or creating a user with it; - changing the Administrator's e-mail, deactivating, resetting, signing out, revoking with removePasskeys, changing the default company, unblocking or deleting the Administrator; - deleting or changing the system role. ## 2026-10-17T18:28:31Z · p01-odoo-rig · round 12 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `738eb551399f609759ac49364a99fe8b446e2d03`
- Critic: fresh critic, p01-odoo-rig round 12
- Biggest gap: **Traced causation can still be laundered: a message the page did not time itself (a web-socket push the server times) is credited to the click just before it** — Round 12 traces each write back through Chromium's task record. A task 'of no traceable origin' (a message) counts as the step's when two things hold: the page task just before it was caused, within REACTION_SECONDS, and no uncaused page task ran in between. That guard only looks at the page's own main thread and its workers. Timing that lives outside the thread is invisible to it.  W1 shows this with a web socket. Set-up turns on a setting. The stand-in pushes 'show' (a Close button appears), then on its own server timer pushes 'go' 500 ms later, and the page approves. One click on Close sits between the two pushes. Nothing in the page ran between the click's input task and the 'go' message, so the message, and the approve write it sends, are credited to the click. The run verifies with 1 step, 0 keys and 0.6-1.4 s, and beats Odoo's follow-approval baseline in 3 of 3 runs. Web sockets are allowed as background channels, so nothing else refuses it. This is the off-the-clock class of rounds 8-11 (S1/S3) again, moved off the page's thread.  A second, smaller hole: the delay limits do not add up. A 1.9 s held answer plus a 0.9 s timer gives a 2.8 s delay after a click on plain text, and it is accepted (W3), while S10 refuses 2.5 s.  Possible fixes: (a) Credit a message task only when its origin is traced. For web-socket and event-stream messages, use the trace's WebSocket and EventSource receive events, and link them only to a frame the page sent from a caused task. Otherwise treat any 'other' task as uncaused while a long-lived channel is open, or for any message event whose source is not in the page's own thread. (b) Bound the total delay along a chain, from the step's input to the write, not each link alone. For example, chain time at most LINK_SECONDS. (c) Add W1 and W3 as self-tests and mutations. (d) Add self-tests for my missed mutations K6, K8 and K9: a browser key counts its keys, human_plus_wait = human + wait in the summary, and the review page's letters come from assignLetters at random.

RESULT lines (plants/critic-r12-plants-run1.log): - W1: steps=1 keys=0 machine=0.596, browser_record {caused: true, chain: ['a DevTools command of step 1', 'a task that followed at once (a message or an observer)']}. - W3: steps=1 keys=0 machine=2.925, chain ['input of step 1', 'the answer to GET /api/tick19 after 1.904 s', 'a timer of 0.9 s']. ## 2026-10-18T00:28:27Z · p02-tenancy · round 11 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `e9cfec4fd19150cd24539dba295d15cb8fe9e8b1`
- Critic: fresh critic, p02-tenancy round 11
- Biggest gap: **A role held in one company acts on another company's records: Company manager in ALN-DXB, Read-only in ALN-FZE, changes FZE's company and branches and creates FZE branches while working in DXB** — Permissions for a request are the workspace roles plus the roles of the working company. The company scope that RLS enforces is every company the user works in. No tenancy endpoint (companies update and logo, branches create and update, and access update too) checks the permission in the company of the record it touches, and the kernel's SaveChanges guard checks only that the company is in scope. So a group's 'accountant in the Dubai LLC, read-only in the JAFZA entity' (the case per-company roles were built for) can rewrite the JAFZA entity's legal registration (trade licence, TRN, the Arabic-legal-name setting, active flag), its branches, and create new branches in it. Switching to the company where they hold the role is all it takes. The screen offers it too: the FZE form opens with Save while the user works in DXB. G2CompanyRoleTests checks only a user who is working in Y, so every gate passes. Fix: authorise company-owned writes and reads against the grants held in the record's own company, for example a kernel check that every ICompanyOwned row written in a request lies in a company where the declared permission is covered (UserGrants.Covers(permission, companyId)), or narrow the session's write scope to the companies where the permission is held. Add the gate: for every endpoint and permission held only in X, a user working in X who names Y's records (path ids, body companyId) gets 403 and changes nothing. Plant 'permission checked in the working company only' in the gate self-tests. The screens should also offer edit only where the role is held.
- Gap evidence: Run ERP_PROJECT=x ERP_HTTP_PORT=20250 ERP_DB_PORT=20251 ./erp up, then node attack/cross-company-role.mjs http://localhost:20250 and node attack/cross-company-more.mjs. Results (cross-company-role.log): working in ALN-FZE, PUT branch and PUT company give 403. After switching to ALN-DXB, PUT /api/tenancy/branches/{JAFZA-WH} gives 200 and PUT /api/tenancy/companies/{ALN-FZE} gives 200 (tradeLicenceNumber JAFZA-178204 -> X-ALN-DXB-6395). Then POST /api/tenancy/branches {companyId: ALN-FZE} gives 201 (code SIDE). Audit rows 202124 and 202125 name accountant@alnoor.example (attack/cross-company-audit.txt). Shot 01 shows the FZE form editable with Save while the user works in DXB. The unplanted ./erp verify passed (1665/1665), so no gate catches it.
- Hard gates:

In the seeded demo, accountant@alnoor.example holds 'Company manager' (tenancy.companies.update, branches.create/update) only in ALN-DXB and 'Read-only' in ALN-FZE. While working in FZE, PUT /companies/{FZE} and PUT /branches/{FZE branch} answer 403, which is correct. After switching to DXB, the same user's PUT /api/tenancy/companies/{FZE id} answers 200: the trade licence changed from JAFZA-178204 to X-ALN-DXB-6395. PUT /api/tenancy/branches/{JAFZA-WH} answers 200 (city changed). POST /api/tenancy/branches {companyId: FZE} answers 201 (branch SIDE created in FZE). Audit rows 202124 and 202125 name the accountant. The screen agrees with the API: the FZE company form opens editable with Save while the user works in DXB (shot 01). The session's permissions are the working company's grants, while the company scope (RLS) is every company the user works in. No endpoint checks the permission in the record's own company, so 'every action is denied unless a role grants it' is broken on this piece's endpoints. G2CompanyRoleTests only checks a user who is working in Y, never a user working in X who names Y's record, so the whole gate suite passes.

- Biggest gap: **The print layout base prints the keyboard focus ring: a keyboard user's printout carries a 2 px blue outline on whatever control had the focus** — The shell is keyboard-first, so a user nearly always has the focus on a control when they press Ctrl+P or pick 'Print this screen'. The print stylesheet does not turn focus styling off. In print media the focused element keeps its 'solid 2px rgb(26,115,232)' outline, so the printed Arabic users list shows a blue box around a column header ('آخر تسجيل دخول' in 07-print-users-ar.jpg). This print base is what every later report will use: invoices, delivery notes and statements a UAE trading company hands to customers. A visible UI artefact on those documents makes them look unfinished, and Odoo's printed reports do not carry one. Everything else in the piece holds: every gate caught every plant, and ours beats Odoo on all four compared tasks. Fix: in the shared print stylesheet, set outline: none and box-shadow: none on :focus and :focus-visible, and blur nothing (the focus must come back after printing). ## 2026-10-18T03:59:07Z · p00-foundation · round 11 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `562f649b8f296431b83cb120ee34acdf32553f55`
- Critic: fresh critic, p00-foundation round 11
- Biggest gap: **The permission gate (G2) never calls an endpoint with the caller's own id, so a kernel fault that lets anyone act on their own record passes every test** — Every G2 matrix (A_user_whose_roles_grant_nothing_is_denied_everything, Granting_exactly_one_permission_opens_exactly_the_endpoints_that_declare_it and the carrier matrices) builds its requests with G2PermissionTests.Request. That method fills each route value with Guid.NewGuid() and each body with {}. So a permission decision that depends on route values is never tested: the caller's own user id, a record the caller created, or the caller's own company. Plant P6 in the kernel PermissionAuthorizationHandler passes any request whose id or userId is the caller's own. This is a common real-world shortcut ('people may always manage their own profile'). With it, a user with no roles read their own user record, access and sign-in history, and PUT their own user record (200: display name and language changed). Every gate and module test still passed, about 640 tests across G2, Rules, G1, SelfTests, Kernel and modules. For a UAE trading company, self-service screens are next: My payslip, my expense claims, my leave, my approvals. Each is an action on 'my own' record, and this gate would wave through a missing permission check on all of them. Fix: in G2, add the caller's own ids (user id, own session, a record the caller created, the caller's company and branch) to the route values each deny and grant-one matrix tries, next to the random id. Ratchet the count of self-referencing requests.
- Gap evidence: Reproduce: apply plants/plants.diff (P6 hunk in src/Kernel/Erp.Kernel/Security/Permissions.cs) and add plants/CriticR11Proof.cs and plants/CriticR11Escalation.cs under tests/Erp.Gates.Tests/Critic/. Then run plants/run-in-toolbox.sh with PLANTS='P6PROOF P6NONE P6' (and run-in-toolbox-2.sh with 'P6ESC P6G1 P6M'). Results: CriticR11Proof fails only with CRITIC_PLANT=P6 (GET /api/identity/users/{self} -> 200 for noaccess@alpha.example). CriticR11Escalation shows 'PUT /api/identity/users/{self} displayName -> 200 ... "displayName":"Changed without permission","language":"ar"'. G2+Rules: 86 tests, exit 0 (plant-P6.log). Kernel and modules: 452 tests, exit 0 (plant-P6M.log). The cause is at tests/Erp.Gates.Tests/G2/G2PermissionTests.cs:1037: endpoint.Path(_ => Guid.NewGuid().ToString()).

- Other findings:
  - The kernel Money type's invariants can be bypassed. This is the most important finding after the gate miss. Round 10's rounding gap is fixed: three lines of USD 1.00 at 3.6725 now total AED 11.02 (money-probe/output.txt). But Money is a public readonly record struct with public init setters, so these all compile and run without validation: `new Money { Amount = 100, Currency = "USD", ExchangeRate = 0, BaseAmount = 999 }`, `line with { BaseAmount = 50 }`, and JsonSerializer.Deserialize<Money> with the platform's own JSON options. The JSON read accepted currency 'usd', rate -2 and base 1 for amount 10.123456, and Add on the result returned -40.49 without complaint. Once p08 and the ledger accept Money in request bodies, an amount's stored base amount and rate can disagree with each other (CLAUDE.md rule 2). Fix: make the setters private, add a JsonConverter that goes through Create or Unrounded, and add a test (money-probe/Program.cs reproduces).
  - [route: p03-identity] The users list answers a user's roleIds in no fixed order. RolesOf in src/Modules/Identity/Erp.Modules.Identity/Users/UserEndpoints.cs:678 has no ORDER BY. Two identical requests listed the same two role ids in opposite orders, and the G1 differential oracle reported this as 'unexpected oracle'. That failed GateSelfTests.The_HTTP_attack_catches_planted_header_route_and_body_leaks in the critic's P6 gate run (plant-P6G1.log: the first difference is only the roleIds order). This can turn a verify red at random. Sort roleIds, or have the gate's normaliser treat id arrays as sets.
  - Plants L6 and L7 were caught. L6, list totals kept in a singleton's instance field, was caught statically by G1ProcessStateTests and live by G1NonInterference and G1HttpIsolation. L7, erp.current_tenant_id() without the transaction marker, was caught live by G1DatabaseIsolationTests (plants/plant-results.txt).
  - Fixed since round 10: sign-in lockout now says 'Too many failed sign-ins with this e-mail address from this device. Sign-in with it from here is paused until 4:23 AM (about 15 minutes)...', and the right password while paused gets the same message (10-lockout-message.jpg). On the Arabic sign-in screen the password's show button now sits at the left (end) edge (03-sign-in-ar.jpg).
  - Still open from round 10: the empty workspace's first administrator signs in with the operator-set initial password and lands in the shell with no prompt to choose their own (browser-notes-2.txt).
  - English and Arabic: the sign-in screen and shell switch to rtl/ar. In the Arabic shell the only Latin text is the company codes, 'Ctrl' and the 'English' switch. Keyboard sign-in by the domain address works, and 40 Tab presses never stop on the document body. The read-only role sees no New button and gets 403 on POST /api/identity/roles. The no-roles user sees no menu and gets 403 (browser-notes*.txt).
  - Processor time: verify used 10,250 s against the 10,500 s maximum, a 2.4% margin, with load up to 92 while two other verifies ran. Round 10 used 9,512 s. The margin is thin and rising with each round's added tests.
  - The critic's attack checksum (from round 10) compared raw psql output, so the order of stderr and stdout produced a false 'B checksum changed' on the first run (attack/run1). Fixed in this round's copy by comparing NOTICE lines only.
  - erp_auth (the sign-in resolver) reads identity.users, sessions and user_credentials of every tenant by policy auth_resolver_read, by design. It cannot read roles or audit. Noted for future reviews of that role.

## 2026-10-18T07:40:19Z · p05-list-search · round 12 · BLOCKED

- Commit judged: `ebb489721587d69e36640af6d56bb575f3672848`
- Critic: fresh critic, p05-list-search round 12
- Biggest gap: **At volume, the list gate only judges one-letter searches. A tenant-blind total for broad multi-letter searches (initials path), kept on disk, passes all of G1** — p05 promises that G1 attacks every list registered with the framework, and every later 100,000-row list relies on that. After round 9, ListVolume judges each list at demo volume. Its searches are one-letter words only: 14 letters, plus sorted and grouped versions of them. The engine runs different code for other search shapes. One word of 2 to 5 Latin letters takes the initials path (InitialsWord and InitialsMatch: an extra regular-expression condition and an initials score). A broad answer (over MaxRankedRows) also has its own branch. Neither runs at volume in any gate, and the small-volume gates never get a broad answer. The structural checks only look at process memory (ProcessState), and the process-global rule in TenantSource does not cover the file system. So a total remembered in the temp folder, keyed without the tenant, is invisible to every check. That is a natural optimisation of the slowest answer while someone types ('ma' reads the regular expression on about 30,000 rows). Plant L13 does exactly that. Fixes: (a) Build the volume query set from the engine's branches, not from letters. Include one word of each length from 2 to MaxInitials that is broad (over MaxRankedRows) in both tenants with different counts, and one that is ranked. Add multi-word searches (broad and ranked), Arabic words of 2 or more letters, and digit searches. Send each as B, A, B, A. Report 'blind' for any branch with no discriminating query, as is already done for Unranked and Ranked. (b) Give TenantSource a reviewed rule for file-system and other external state in product code: System.IO File, Directory and FileStream writes, Path.GetTempPath, MemoryMappedFile, named Mutex or Semaphore. (c) Add the L13 shape to the ListVolume self-test, next to the existing 'total memo for big filtered queries'.

- Other findings:
  - Plant P10, a screen-permission miss on p05's own list component, is the same kind of fault as the G2 fail recorded under hard_gates. ListView.tsx canChange becomes 'isMine \|\| isShared', dropping the canShare check, so a reader without lists.views.share is offered 'Update this view' and 'Delete this view' on the workspace's shared views. All 504 web unit tests pass with it. The e2e test for the reader only asserts that the save dialog has no share box. The server still refuses the write, so no data changes. Fix: the screen sweep, or a ListView unit test, should open a shared view as a reader with canShare false and assert that neither action is offered.
  - Plant P11 has two variants. The first, POST /views/{id}/share turning the caller's own view into a shared one in place, failed G2's list-view test and G1 HttpIsolation only by side effect: the administrator's later DELETE and B's later PUT on that view got 404 (run-L13-P11-g1-g2.log). The copy variant P11b is caught only by the reviewed list read-permission-writes.txt. The behavioural list-view check passed. It sends the reader's writes only to POST /views and PUT /views/{id}, and never sends a POST route with an id carrying the reader's own view id. G2's derived permission rule also accepts any route under /api/lists/{key}/ that does not start with 'shared-views' as correctly guarded by the list's read permission, without review. If a reviewer accepted a read-permission-writes line ('a reader shares their own view'), nothing behavioural would catch it. Fix: send every write route under /api/lists/{key}/ (any method, any shape) as a reader without share, using the reader's own view ids, then assert that no shared view appears or changes.

- Biggest gap: **The G1 gates never send the partial preferences body the shell itself sends, so a cross-tenant leak on the language switch (Alt+L sends only {language}) passes every check** — Every G1 check that exercises the shell's own write, PUT /api/identity/me/preferences, sends a full body with both language and numerals: ShellAnswers.PreferenceVariants covers the 4 combinations, and TenantActivity's first and last values fill every enumerated field. The product never sends that body on its main path. Alt+L, the top-bar button and My account send {language} only, and the digits switch sends {numerals} only. So any behaviour that differs when a field is left out is outside G1. I planted SL1: the endpoint remembers the digits last chosen by anyone, in any tenant, in a file, and answers a language-only switch with them. G1NonInterference, G1ShellAnswers (3 tests), G1HttpIsolation and G1ProcessState all passed. On the running planted build, tenant A's answer revealed tenant B's last digit choice. Real code produces exactly this pattern: 'fill unspecified fields from a shared default or cache', partial-update merges, and PATCH-style endpoints that every later module (invoices, stock, journals) will have. Under the round rules, a gate that misses a fault planted in this piece's surface fails the round. Fix: derive write variants from the request the product's own client sends, not only from full enumerations. For every body schema with optional or nullable fields, add each single-field variant and the empty-but-valid subsets to the turns that NonInterference, ShellAnswers and TenantActivity run. For the preferences endpoint, include {language} alone and {numerals} alone, in English and Arabic sessions. Add a self-test plant shaped like SL1 so the gate proves it catches it.

The new gate does not defend that fix against the next regression of the same kind. CompanyRoleRecords sends every company-Y record a body from G1WriteOracle.Valid, which sets every boolean to true. - Biggest gap: **The one command (./erp verify) is at its processor-time limit: one of this round's two clean-clone runs failed G3 on it** — Every gate held and every test passed. What holds p00 back is that its own one command now fails the owner's processor maximum on a busy machine. Run 1 on the clean clone passed all 1,746 tests and was still FAILED by the ratchet: 10,726 processor seconds against the 10,500 s maximum. Run 2, on a slightly quieter machine, passed with 10,433 s, a 0.6% margin. Round 11 used 10,250 s; this branch's own integration runs used 10,338 to 10,701 s. Each round adds tests: dotnet 647 to 696 and e2e 106 to 111 since round 11. The cost by stage: dotnet 7,987-8,136 s (77%), web 1,954-2,068 s, e2e 443-472 s (nearly double r11's 253 s). So within a round or two the clean-clone gate will fail by default, and every later piece (accounting, inventory, VAT, payroll) will start out BLOCKED on a gate it cannot affect. For a UAE trading or manufacturing company this is the gate that lets new modules be built and checked at all. Fix it inside p00's surface without lowering the bar: profile where the dotnet stage spends its processor time (the G1 HTTP isolation, non-interference and per-plant self-test fixtures each start whole hosts and databases). Share fixtures and test databases across the gate collections, and run each planted self-test only against the endpoints its plant can reach.   - The plants also showed the gate machinery's static layers are strong. A plant that sets the tenant from a permission check is flagged by G1TenantSource and G1TenantSettingCode ('names the tenant setting ... only reviewed methods may'). A process-wide dictionary is flagged by G1ProcessState. A kernel endpoint with no permission stops the host from starting.
  - [route: p03-identity] POST /api/identity/roles/{id}/copy needs only identity.roles.create. A user who cannot read roles can copy any role whose permissions they hold, and the 201 answer shows that role's permission set. Probing ids also tells a role that exists (copied, or 403) from one that does not (404). This is low risk because the permissions are a subset of the caller's own, but it is a read without roles.read (plant-PROOFNONE.log: 'POST copy -> 201' for a create-only user).
  - erp_app can read pg_class through column-level grants but not reltuples, relpages or pg_stats, so row counts across tenants are not exposed (db-app-role-attack.txt). erp_auth reads identity.users of every tenant (101,016 rows) by design, for sign-in, and is refused on roles and audit.

Keyboard sign-in works in both languages. Ctrl+K, typing الأدوار and Enter opens the roles screen. The no-roles user sees no menu and gets 403 from the API. The read-only user sees no New button and gets 403 on POST roles. After sign-out the token answers 401 as Bearer and as cookie (browser-notes.txt).

## 2026-10-18T20:40:23Z · p06-form-report · round 8 · WIN

- Commit judged: `2df8759d1a3b00b481174a7909a85c12626c1375`
- Critic: fresh critic, p06-form-report round 8
- Biggest gap: **Printed documents stop at 2,000 rows: a long list or report cannot be printed or saved as a PDF whole** — The form now edits in fewer actions than Odoo, and every compared print task wins. What most limits the piece for a UAE trading or manufacturing company is what it can put on paper. Every PDF, on-screen document and print (ReportEngine.DocumentRowLimit = 2000) stops at the first 2,000 rows. It says so ('The first 2,000 of 100,007 rows; narrow the parameters to print the rest'), but it gives no way to print the rest. Only CSV/XLSX carry up to 200,000 rows. A stock list, price list, customer statement, or an aged list for an auditor or a free-zone authority routinely runs past 2,000 lines, and it is wanted as one signed PDF in English or Arabic, not as an XLSX or a set of narrowed prints. Every later module (sales, purchasing, inventory, accounting) inherits this cap through the report framework. Needed: a PDF/print path that renders the whole selection, for example streamed or paged rendering, or a background job for large documents. The run must stay read-only and tenant-scoped, and the document must say how many rows it holds.
- Gap evidence: Screenshot 06 (gauntlet/evidence/p06-form-report/r8/browser/06-en-report-grouped-totals.jpg): Reports > Users by role > Show, signed in as admin@alnoor.example on the seeded demo (ERP_SEED_USERS_CSV=gauntlet/compare/data/out/users.csv ./erp up). The document reads 'The first 2,000 of 100,007 rows; narrow the parameters to print the rest.' To reproduce through the API: GET /api/reports/run/identity.usersByRole?format=pdf&language=ar prints 2,000 rows; format=xlsx carries all of them. The cap is DocumentRowLimit = 2000 in src/Modules/Reports/Erp.Modules.Reports/ReportEngine.cs, also noted by the round 6 critic.

## 2026-10-19T03:38:25Z · p03-identity · round 12 · WIN

- Commit judged: `2d46250b5d2a1154bac1579f9e924d834ed29435`
- Critic: fresh critic, p03-identity round 12
- Biggest gap: **In the shipped deployment every client still has the gateway's address. Five anonymous wrong passwords keep a password user out on any new device for 15 minutes, and 30 anonymous requests a minute stop every sign-in in every workspace** — Round 11's gap is only partly fixed. Two things now work, and the administrator can see what is happening: - A valid set-up code signs in while the gateway address is paused (attacks/setup-code-paused.txt: 409 'choose a new password', then 200). - A browser that signed in before (device cookie) still signs in (200). - The sign-in history and the paused list label 172.22.0.1 'shared gateway' in English and 'بوابة مشتركة' in Arabic. They tell the admin to give a new set-up code (screenshots 04 and 05).  The root cause is unchanged: the one-command deployment does not see real client addresses, and the fix covers only two kinds of client. An anonymous script sends 5 wrong passwords for the finance manager's e-mail. After that, their right password from a new laptop, a fresh browser or cleared cookies answers 429 for 15 minutes (Retry-After 900). The message says 'Too many failed sign-ins ... from this device', although that device never failed (screenshot 12). The script can repeat this every 15 minutes, indefinitely.  The only way back in is for an administrator to reset the user's password to a set-up code each time. That throws away their password, and it still does not help on the next new device.  Worse, and new evidence this round: the sign-in rate limit (Erp:RateLimits:SignInPerMinute, 30, partitioned by remote address in ErpPlatform) also sees one client. 30 anonymous requests to /api/auth/sign-in at unknown addresses in 3.5 s were enough. After them, the next correct sign-ins of admin@gulfsteel.example and admin@alnoor.example from other browsers both answered 429 request.tooMany (attacks/global-rate.txt). In the shipped deployment, one script stops every user of every workspace from signing in. Without any attacker, a 40-person trading company signing in at 8 a.m. would also hit it.  UAE trading and manufacturing companies often sit behind one office NAT and log in at shift start. Odoo behind its usual proxy setup does not fall into this, so this is the one thing that most holds p03 back.  Fix: - Ship the compose deployment with a reverse proxy that forwards the client address, with ERP_KNOWN_PROXIES set by default. Or publish the port with host networking or a proxy that preserves the source. - Partition both the pause and the rate limit by a stronger client key when the address is shared. - Add a test: behind one shared address, a second client with no device cookie still signs in with its right password after another client's 5 failures and 30 requests.
- Gap evidence: 1. ./erp up (any ports), then the probes: - node attacks/pause-dos.mjs: 'the owner from a new laptop (no device cookie), right password: 429 retry-after 900 ... from this device'. Paused clients: [{source: 172.22.0.1, kind: gateway, failures: 5}]. - node attacks/setup-code-paused.mjs: the invitee's set-up code gives 409 then 200. A known device gives 200. 'viewer from a new laptop, right password: 429 retry-after 900'. - node attacks/global-rate.mjs: '30 sign-in attempts at unknown addresses in 3.5 s: {401: 30}', then 'admin@gulfsteel.example (right password, another browser): 429 request.tooMany' and 'admin@alnoor.example ...: 429'. 2. Browser: node walks/w7.mjs. Screenshot 12 shows the finance manager on a fresh browser paused 'from this device' after 5 failures by 'curl/8.5.0'.
